25 Years Later, ‘Buffy the Vampire Slayer’s Best Quote Is Still the Most Inspiring Line in TV History

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Buffy’s story has always revolved around death. Throughout its seven-season run, Buffy Summers (Sarah Michelle Gellar) has wanted to escape from it or, boldly, return to it. Upon a rewatch, death is one of the clearest themes of the series — this wasn’t the case on my first time watching, when I reacted in disbelief every time the series brought up the possibility of Buffy dying. Surely, they wouldn’t go there. But they did.

In the Buffy the Vampire Slayer season 5 finale, Sunnydale is collapsing, and Buffy’s allies have nowhere to go. Her little sister, Dawn (Michelle Trachtenberg), is the reason why a dangerous portal has opened, and she tries to jump in to close it. But Buffy stops her. “The hardest thing in this world is to live in it,” she tells her. “Be brave. Live. For me.”

The hardest thing in this world is to live in it.

The two share the same blood, so Buffy is capable of closing the portal on her own. She jumps in and dies. It’s a moment that closes out an arc that had been teased since season 1.

Season 5 is one of Buffy’s most emotional seasons. It’s also one of its boldest, committing to a new central dynamic: Buffy and Dawn. Maligned series creator Joss Whedon shared that he introduced Dawn as a way of giving Buffy an intense dynamic that wasn’t based in romance. After seasons of Angel, Riley, and hints of Spike, it feels like a breath of fresh air to see Buffy get mad, angry, happy, and distraught over a different type of relationship.

One of the show’s biggest twists was the introduction of Dawn. Buffy, a notorious single child for most of the show, suddenly has a sister, introduced in a throwaway moment that made me think I had skipped a few episodes. But Dawn’s introduction is not a plot hole. The season spends the rest of its runtime deconstructing this, revealing that Dawn is a Key made up of ancient energy that was put into the body of a girl, with monks giving Buffy and her friends memories of her.

With Dawn’s arrival, Buffy’s era as a teenager is officially over. The season is all about making her grow up fast, stripping her of almost everything that gave her a sense of safety, including her human boyfriend Riley and her mother Joyce. It’s the end of Buffy the Vampire Slayer’s lighter era. From then on, you can’t say that the series is about a scrappy group of teens trying to save the world.

“The Gift,” written by and directed by Whedon, neatly closes all of the storylines that it introduced in its first episode. Throughout the season Buffy and Dawn’s dynamic morphs, from annoying siblings to Buffy becoming Dawn’s caregiver. Buffy learns that Dawn isn’t real early on, but the discovery changes nothing. To Buffy, Dawn is the most important person in her life and only family left. In the finale, when Giles brings up the possibility of sacrificing Dawn to stop a ritual, Buffy immediately shuts him down.

“The Gift” takes its title from a line in “Intervention,” where Buffy goes on a vision quest to meet the First Slayer, a prehistoric girl who tells her: “Death is your gift.” The line reinforces one of Buffy’s biggest fears: that she’s a killing machine, closer to the vampires and demons she hunts than to the human world she’s so desperate to belong to. But Buffy’s words to Dawn show what her gift really means. For Buffy, being the Slayer has never been about strength or power; it’s about sacrifice. With her death, Buffy tasks her sister with living, no matter how difficult and lonely it gets. Those words will haunt her in season 6, finding Buffy at her most anguished and destructive after being ripped out of heaven and coming back to life.

While we now know that Buffy the Vampire Slayer continued for two more seasons and that it ended on a relatively happy note (at least she’s alive), the writers didn’t know that. Season 5 was the last to air on The WB, before the series found its final home on UPN. That uncertainty is why Whedon and company treated “The Gift” as a series finale, wrapping up Buffy’s story while engaging in a conversation with “Prophecy Girl,” the season 1 finale. That episode shows a younger Buffy, one who’s so afraid of dying that she can barely face the Master. This time around, Buffy chooses death calmly, because it means saving her sister.

“The Gift” ends with an indelible image. As Buffy’s friends mourn her and Dawn remembers her sister’s final words, we pan over Buffy’s body over a pile of rubble. We cut to her tombstone: “Buffy Anne Summers. Beloved sister. Devoted friend. She saved the world a lot.”

Epic Games Launcher is coming to SteamOS

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Epic Games confirmed that its Linux launcher will officially support SteamOS and Steam Decks.

Epic Games confirmed that its Linux launcher will officially support SteamOS and Steam Decks.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

In October, Epic Games said that it was “investigating” making Fortnite compatible with Valve’s Linux-based SteamOS. At the time, Epic Games Launcher was in preview, but SteamOS was not specifically listed as a supported distro. Now, Communications Director Jake Jones has confirmed SteamOS compatibility, along with a handful of other distros, to GamingOnLinux. In response to a question about the AppImage being replaced with Debian / Ubuntu .deb downloads, Jones said:

“The new Epic Games Launcher is still in Preview and we plan to support as many Linux distributions as possible, including Fedora, SteamOS, Cachy, Omarchy, and more. We’re testing and evaluating packaging formats for distribution, and we’ll restore AppImage this month.”

That doesn’t necessarily mean Fortnite will come to SteamOS. Easy Anti-Cheat (EAC) Linux v2 support still remains an open question. When it announced its work to bring Fortnite to Linux, the company said that the Steam Deck “does not support the required features for our EAC v2 solution, but we are actively talking with Valve and hope to support it in the future.”

Watch The 2027 Hyundai Tucson Get Hurled Around The Nürburgring Like A Huge Miata

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

There's nothing quite like watching a vehicle get driven in ways you never expected. Think Miatas on a rally stage in Finland or a bunch of smoky pickup trucks on a race track in Thailand. There's something gratifying about seeing things taken to new extremes, and we probably all have a story or two about ripping a beater or rental car in ways that most wouldn't.

But if you're a test driver, it's your duty to drive cars, trucks, and SUVs in ways most wouldn't. And if you're working for Hyundai, that means hurling a 2027 Tucson, before it goes on sale, around the Nürburgring Nordschleife — seemingly the world's most famous test track — like a huge Miata. Sure, Porsche, BMW, Audi, and Mercedes-Benz test drivers probably have a great time wringin' out their steeds on this German track's sacred surface, but we bet the Hyundai cats are having just as much (or possibly more) fun.

It isn't all a big joke, though, because manufacturers have to explore a vehicle's limits to truly poke and prod it for deficiencies. But it looks freaking epic. Man, what we'd give to sit shotgun on this stilted-up rollercoaster ride.

What makes testing cars at the Nürburgring so special? Well, for one, claiming a fastest production car record. There's a good chance that this isn't the case with the flyin' 2027 Hyundai Tucson, but hey, it's probably a good idea to keep an idea on the company's upcoming news coverage just in case.

However, more realistic: there's the fact that it's a very long (almost 13 miles) and variety-rich track with big undulations, flowing sweepers, big elevation changes, on- and off-camber bits, high-speed sections, jumps, as well as the ability to be bone-dry on one end and raining on the other. Germany's Eiffel, where the 'Ring is located, is a climate that's rich in rain that comes and goes in the blink of an eye. Testing the Tucson's suspension, brakes, and their relationship with the tires' grip here gives engineers a good idea where the limits are in a bunch of scenarios. Additionally, the high speed gives them an idea of how well the cooling system works, how well the brakes work, as well as if the engine and transmission will have any issues. If it can survive a flying lap here, it's definitely good for service in normal, everyday driving.

Plus, it helps that Hyundai maintains a facility right nearby. The brand tunes all of its high-performance N models on the 'Ring, which undergo stricter and more extensive testing than non-performance fare. The plain ol' Tucson may not get that same exact treatment (yet), but it's cool to see them still driving 'em on there like they stole 'em

Manchester United v Aston Villa: Premier League – live

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

⚽️ Premier League updates from 4.30pm GMT ⚽️ Jonathan Wilson: There are few reasons for Emery to leave Villa for United ⚽️ Today’s results | Mail Tim

“The League should do something about it,” says Loig Allix Van Thi. About what? “Jackson’s throat-slash signature celebration is just unacceptable. End of.”

“At the stroke of halftime,” says Peter Oh, “the Telemundo broadcast I’m watching here in California showed an ad for a global fast food chain touting a new menu item called a Crunchadilla. Either my glasses aren’t on or my faith in Unai Emery is at a low ebb because I read it as Crunchdavilla.”

That was a half of two halves. Villa roared into a ridiculously easy 2-0 lead, thanks to Alysson, Nicolas Jackson and some poor keeping from Senne Lammens. But then United started playing quite well. They got back into the game by their preferred route, a Bruno Fernandes penalty, and then Youri Tielemans made it 2-2 by scoring his first goal for his new team, against his old one.

It’s been mad. It’s been Manchester United.

45+3 min Has Fernandes conceded a pen here? No – it was a high boot, and Alysson went down in a heap, but the VAR cleared it swiftly.

45+2 min Big chance for Villa! Goretzka sends a through ball to Alysson, whose neat dink over Lammens goes just wide.

45+1 min There will be three added minutes after this one.

A left-foot thump, a first goal for United for Tielemans, and United are out of jail for now!

43 min Chance for Amad! Villa lose the ball playing out and Amad’s shot is well saved by Suzuki, when it should be nestling in the far corner. But then …

42 min Yoro makes not one neat interception but two, turning defence into attack, but nothing comes of either. The camera finds Robin van Persie, looking distinguished.

40 min McGinn’s chip finds the head of Pau Torres, but he sends the ball over the bar.

38 min Manzambi wins a free kick on the corner of the box as Mazraoui makes a clumsy challenge.

35 min Tielemans, who is coming into the game, wins a couple of duels against his old mates. A moment later he plays a crisp pass to Amad, whose shot from the right goes skimming past the far post.

35 min Amad shows his brighter side by running onto a lovely chip from Mainoo, but he can’t quite pluck it out of the air.

34 min Amad fouls Maatsen rather needlessly, but in Villa’s half. In the stands, green and gold scarves are being brandished.

33 min A caption informs us about United’s home record against Villa in the league since 1956. Won 43, lost three. They’ll be doing well if they keep that going.

32 min Fernandes’ free kick eludes everybody, though Suzuki may have got a touch that went undetected. Villa get up the other end, where Mainoo is needed to put in a sliding tackle on someone.

30 min Mbeumo wins a free kick, 25 yards out on the right. And United have now gone a full 25 minutes without conceding.

28 min It’s breathless stuff now. United counter and Fernandes fizzes in a fine cross-or-shot, which Suzuki gets down to save. And then Amad goes just wide with a calm side-footer from 12 yards out.

27 min Another chance for United! Good work by Amad down the right and the ball comes out to Fernandes, whose shot is deflected just wide. From the resulting corner, Mbeumo tries a shot which is tipped wide by Suzuki. From the corner after that, Mainoo’s shot draws a save from Suzuki.

24 min Chance for United! Tielemans sends a classy switch over to Cunha, who takes one of his better long shots – curling in at the far post, until Suzuki tips it round the post. From the resulting corner, Suzuki fumbles the ball but gets away with it.

24 min Fans have not been leaving in droves, as far as I can see.

23 min Maatsen bursts down the left, only to be stopped by Amad.

21 min Jackson, showing the uses of a No 9, holds the ball up, then threatens to run in behind before being seen out.

20 min Fernandes, on the right, tries to slip in Tielemans, who may not be much enjoying his reunion so far.

19 min If any fans are planning to leave after 21 minutes, they’ll have got their money’s worth.

18 min Matheus Cunha’s week so far: two goals scored, two penalties won.

The pen is confirmed. Two-nil down and getting a pen … ring a bell?

Cunha is brought down by Kamara, and Chris Kavanagh points to the spot.

7 min …but Villa break again and Alysson’s latest shot is deflected for a corner.

6 min Breaking news: United are in Villa’s half.

Lammens can only fend a shot to Nicolas Jackson, who has a tap-in. That 11-goal defeat is now a distinct possibility.

4 min The attack went down the left before a simple switch found Alysson in space, helped by a mis-chest from Mazraoui. Now Villa are on the left again, with a free kick…

2 min Alysson’s shot from the right went through Senne Lammens’ legs.

1 min Villa kick off and go back to the keeper, Suzuki.

The silence gives way to a warm cheer. And then another refrain of “We want Glazers out!”

Wreaths are laid in the centre circle to mark Remembrance Sunday. “Age shall not weary them,” says the announcer. The Last Post rings out, and the silence is impeccable.

Bruno Fernandes leads United out. It feels as if he is the central figure here, and the key. After acking up the assists at will last season, he hasn’t had one since that win over Ipswich – but he did play better against Roma on Tuesday.

Here’s my colleague Will Unwin, who is at Old Trafford. “‘We don’t want a new stadium, we want new owners’ is the banner hanging in the Stretford End, while green and gold balloons fly around the stand. The fans are chanting for the Glazers and Sir Jim Ratcliffe to depart with some rather fruity language. More protests are expected during the game.”

More on the Stranglers, who are showing impressive staying power given that they only appeared here by mistake. “I think the Stranglers’ tune [sic] that MUFC are looking for here,” says John Stedman, “is Something Better Change.”

“MBM’s curse?” says Dave Estherby. “Nothing, nothing, would be funnier than Utd 1-11 Villa this afternoon...”

Ha, well, one man’s joke is another man’s poison.

More on those United protests. “Not sure if you’re aware, or how widespread it is,” says Jemma Robinson, “but lots of season ticket holders ‘sent back’ their tickets today which is why the match was on general sale (never happens) and there’s also supposed to be a walkout at 21 minutes, so will be interesting to see which one is more successful and whether you’ll see it emptying or an Old Trafford filled with non-regulars.” That last eventuality might not be easy to spot. ”Hoping Carrick does well given he’s one of my Man Utd favourites, but torn as the club ownership/general situation is awful.”

Jemma, I suspect you speak for millions of fans.

Sunderland have clung on to beat Chelsea 1-0, which is a great result for them. And yet another embarrassment for Man United. They now have fewer wins than any other team in the Premier League – just the one, against Ipswich, back in August.

A draw today will take them above Sunderland on goal difference, but they’ll still be in the bottom three. To lift them out of the relegation zone, and the league doldrums, only a win will do. If they manage it, they will go roaring up to 16th.

For Villa, who are 12th as it stands, a draw will be enough to get them into the top half of the table, above Newcastle and Spurs. And a win will launch them into the top eight, above Brentford and Leeds.

The second email is picking up on the first. “I think Rick was actually thinking of ‘No More Heroes' by The Stranglers,” says Will Cook, “when referring to Manchester United.”

Fair enough, in the league – but Matheus Cunha produced some heroics against Roma, didn’t he?

Instant correction! Palace won a penalty in added time to make it 1-3, so United now need to avoid defeat by ten goals to stay off the bottom.

United will not be bottom of the table tonight. They’ve been rescued by their old friends Liverpool, who are now 3-0 up at Selhurst Park thanks to Alexander Isak. Palace’s goal difference has sunk to -15, so United will stay above them as long as they can avoid losing by an 11-goal margin.

The first email comes from Rick Harris. “I imagine Unai Emery as a Stranglers fan,” he says, “singing ‘Should I stay or should I go’ every day on the way to work. Staying at Villa is accepting that he is a second tier manager. Going to United is a gamble but it is his best chance of joining the elite managers club. What’s the worst that can happen? Get sacked after a season and a half and go back to Villareal?”

That’s a good point, and much more tactful than Jamie Carragher. But, at the risk of being pedantic, I thought it was The Clash?

Things must be bad at United: there’s a Booze Boycott. Several groups of fans, including The 1958, have got together to say “Reclaim our colours [the green and gold]. Direct the anger at the ownership.” And… “Do not buy drinks inside Old Trafford.” If you’re there, do let us know whether this is having an impact.

Manchester United (probable 4-2-3-1) Lammens; Dalot, Yoro, Maguire, Mazraoui; Mainoo, Tielemans; Amad, Fernandes, Cunha; Mbeumo.

Subs: Darlow, De Ligt, Heaven, Dorgu, Santos, J Fletcher, Mount, Lacey, Zirkzee.

Aston Villa (4-2-3-1) Suzuki; Lindelof, Harwood-Bellis, Torres, Maatsen; Kamara, Goretzka; Alysson, McGinn, Manzambi; Jackson.

Subs: Bizot, Bogarde, Mings, Cisse, Barkley, Mbaye, Buendia, Hemmings, Madjo.

Unai Emery makes three changes. At the back Taylor Harwood-Bellis, once of Man City, gets his first go for Villa in the league after four midweek games. In midfield Leon Goretzka replaces Joao Gomes, and further forward Alysson comes in for Emi Buendia.

Michael Carrick makes only two changes, both enforced. Noussair Mazraoui replaces the suspended Lisandro Martinez, which probably means a reshuffle with Leny Yoro reverting to his natural habitat at centre-back. Youri Tielemans replaces the injured Carlos Baleba, as he did in midweek before playing his part in United’s revival. The home crowd, already restive enough, will be disappointed not to see Shea Lacey given a start.

Afternoon everyone and welcome to the Lindelof-Tielemans derby. It’s the last Premier League game before the international break, and it could be the last game for Michael Carrick as Manchester United’s head coach. The man who lifted his old club to third is now also the man who has led them to third from bottom. The table giveth and the table taketh away.

Carrick’s overall record is still better than most of the managers he has faced. In the PL table since he returned in January, United are third. Of the 17 clubs to be there last season and this, only four have won half their games in that time – Arsenal and Man City obviously, plus Man United and Brighton.

Carrick’s problem is that United’s form was dismal for seven weeks, from the red card Phil Foden was given in the Manchester derby on 13 September to the one Lisandro Martinez was given at Stamford Bridge last weekend. Since then they have mounted a rousing comeback to beat Roma, handing their supporters a sliver of hope. In the league, though, they are still winless since beating Ipswich in August. Their only consolation is that in the recent-form table (last three games for each club), they are not the worst team in Manchester. They have one point, whereas City have none.

Aston Villa, meanwhile, have had a season of three thirds. They started with a whimper, lost three of their first four league games and slumped to 19th. Then they won six out of seven in all competitions and gave everyone else a helpful demonstration of how to beat Man City. But Villa’s last three results have been disappointing, with a draw at home to Fulham sandwiched between batterings at Bournemouth and Barcelona.

Did anyone think either of the bad spells might be the downfall of Unai Emery? They did not. And rightly so, as he has rebuilt his team in no time since losing four stars at once in Morgan Rogers, Ollie Watkins, Youri Tielemans and Ezri Konsa. If pundits speculate about his job it is only to suggest, sometimes unprintably, that he might leave it to join United. That would be the sort of move that was known in Yes, Minister as “brave… very brave”.

The upshot is that, once again, all the pressure is on Carrick. He will be keeping a wary eye on the 2pm scores and praying for a few draws because anything else is lose-lose. United don’t want to see Liverpool or Chelsea pull further away, but they can’t afford to be overtaken by Palace or Sunderland. If they’re bottom of the table tonight, this most likeable of managers will surely be sacked in the morning.

DC's New Lex Luthor Officially Has A Heroic Mission In Dark Reboot Universe

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Back in 2025, DC Comics released a one-shot called Absolute Evil. This is where the most vile villains in the Absolute Universe got together to form their Justice League. While they conducted their meeting, Brainiac was listening and formulating a plan to see how this could meet his needs.

Brainiac saw that each of the people in the conclave had someone to counter them except Jack Grimm (Absolute Joker). Thanks to getting a glimpse into another reality provided by this reality’s Mirror Master, Brainiac had a solution to this problem. He’d get the Lex Luthor of this Earth to join the Justice League. Unfortunately, during Absolute Superman: The Neverending Begins, Luthor turned him down. This led to Brainiac slaughtering Luthor’s family while he was forced to watch.

This moment reminds readers that Brainiac is an unredeemable character. He may have been abused for an untold amount of time, but that’s no excuse for massacring Lex Luthor’s family in front of him. Especially this version of Luthor, who was a family man who was content with living a quiet and peaceful life. Thankfully, some good news comes from this, as Brainiac gets his comeuppance.

While Superman attempted to stop Brainiac from unleashing an onslaught of his clones on the world, Lois Lane went to talk to Lex Luthor. She wanted to know what really happened to his family, and what Brainiac had to do with it. Unsurprisingly, Lex was still in shock. He hadn’t shaved and looked catatonic. What readers find out later is that Luthor may have seemed detached from reality, but he was working on his revenge.

Once Brainiac was in range, Luthor got a notification on his watch. He then shaved his head, equipped himself with two large futuristic-looking guns of his making, somehow got to Brainiac’s ship, and shot his former torturer in the head. Superman was shocked and ordered Lex to drop his weapons. Lex asked who Superman was, shot Kal-El out of the spaceship, and took off his enemy’s vessel.

Superman's most brilliant long-running adversary is moving up not just in power levels, but dark ambitions for the multiverse and all known futures.

It was shocking to see that Brainiac was apparently dead. He failed at making Luthor part of his master plan, instead putting a target on his own head. However, he wasn’t dead. Luthor repairs Brainiac, but only his head. Luthor says that now that he knows his enemy has a family, Brainiac will watch as he kills them. It was an understandable idea, but this may not work out the way Lex hoped.

Brainiac assumed that he could get away with all of his evil deeds because he was always the smartest person in the room. This was justified because, for centuries, he was untouchable. Even when he was under the thumb of Ra’s and Talia al Ghul, there was always a sense that he allowed them to think they were in charge. That hubris led to his downfall and possibly the fall of the other Brainiacs.

The question comic book fans should be wondering is what’s next for Lex Luthor. Can he succeed in killing all the other Brainiacs? If he does, what’s next? Will Luthor become like his Prime-Earth counterpart and have a vendetta against Superman? Or, and this may be the more terrifying option, what if Lex Luthor becomes the next big threat in the Absolute Universe as a new version of Brainiac? If that happens, he could become scarier than Jack Grimm, and that says a lot.

The good news is that it doesn’t have to be all grim. With Absolute Justice coming up, Lex Luthor could be the deciding factor that turns the tide in the big battle. That would be a heck of a twist that no one would see coming. Imagine that. Lex Luthor, and not Superman, being called Savior of the Universe.

8 SNES JRPGs That Reward Players for Exploring the World More Than Grinding Levels

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Murillo Zerbinatto is a JRPG enthusiast, writer, and editor. He has played over 300 RPGs during his gaming career, with 90% of them being JRPGs.

When the JRPG genre was born, its progression was as basic as it gets: kill enemies, gain levels, become stronger. The in-game economy also revolved around earning gold to buy new equipment, but leveling up was the sole driver of power. Many NES and SNES JRPGs demanded some grinding, and since most battles were random encounters, you naturally did it as you moved along.

From classic Final Fantasy to Undertale, these 10 JRPGs reward understanding your enemy more than stacking up levels

Occasionally, however, games emerged that promoted more horizontal progression, focusing less on experience points or level-ups and instead rewarding players who explored every nook and cranny of the world or engaged deeply with unique mechanics introduced in each title. Today, I will explore several SNES JRPGs that reward players for venturing out into the world or leveraging a game's unique feature rather than mindlessly grinding for levels.

No better way to kick things off than with a JRPG that doesn't even feature traditional levels. Illusion of Gaia is part of what is commonly called the Quintet Trilogy, a set of action RPGs crafted with tremendous care, each bringing its own distinct flavor. In Illusion of Gaia, we control Will, who can transform into two other distinct forms at various stages of the adventure: Freedan or Shadow.

To grow stronger, you need to clear out every enemy in a given room, which grants a jewel that permanently increases the protagonist's core attributes. It's a small bit of grind, but instead of defeating monsters repeatedly, you only need to defeat each enemy once per zone. It feels far more gratifying and rewarding because you maintain a clean, consistent sense of progression whenever you choose to clear rooms.

Star Ocean never received an official Western release on the SNES. Yet, it already featured several mechanics that would become defining staples for the franchise, especially if you look at the remake Star Ocean: First Departure. In tri-Ace's JRPG, gaining levels helps because it grants SP to upgrade crafting skills, letting you synthesize equipment far more powerful than anything you can find in shops. In a sense, this rewards players for exploring system mechanics rather than mindlessly slaying mobs.

The JRPG also introduced the Private Action system to build deeper connections with party members, alongside optional character recruitments and a dynamic relationship mechanic. The more you engage with everything the world of Star Ocean has to offer, the greater your rewards will be.

Terranigma is another standout title from the Quintet Trilogy, but unlike Illusion of Gaia, it actually features a traditional leveling system. Defeating enemies yields experience points, which raise attributes on level-up. Still, because Terranigma is an action RPG, execution and player skill matter far more than raw stats, so you never need to grind endlessly just to advance through the story.

Enemies occasionally drop gems you can use to buy new equipment, items, and spells, which is where the magic of exploration takes center stage. You can convert every Magirock you discover into a spell, with more options unlocking as you progress. After casting a spell, the Magirock reverts to its base form, requiring you to slot another spell into it, which directly rewards those who seek out hidden items. On top of that, the town-building sidequests form the emotional heart of Terranigma's narrative, uncovering their full potential only for players willing to track them down.

Breath of Fire is my favorite JRPG series, and each new entry has only refined its craft. While Breath of Fire II on the SNES requires a bit of early farming, especially during the Colosseum events and when Randy joins the party, a specific mechanic will empower your party members far more than any raw level-up: the Shaman system.

After you build your own village and advance through the story, Ryu can recruit Shamans hidden across the world. Using their power, you can fuse one or more Shamans with a party member, altering their form and making them exponentially stronger. For instance, Spar, who feels somewhat underwhelming by default, transforms into an absolute powerhouse through Shaman fusion, with similar massive power spikes applying to Katt and Sten. While levels remain relevant, tracking down Shamans and executing the correct fusions provides a far greater boost to overall combat strength.

The old school villains really were some of the best, as these SNES JRPGs demonstrate.

Character progression in Final Fantasy VI works completely differently depending on who is in your active party. Certain characters like Cyan, Sabin, and Terra acquire new skills and spells simply by leveling up, but others like Edgar, Gau, and Strago require you to explore the world, purchase specialized items, and battle rare monsters. Also, if you want to min-max your stats, you'll want to hold off on leveling until you acquire Espers.

Leveling up in Final Fantasy VI only increases base HP and MP until you unlock Espers. Once acquired, each Esper grants specific attribute bonuses when you level up, letting you tailor and optimize character builds. While min-maxing is not strictly necessary for a casual playthrough, it adds a fascinating layer of depth. Additionally, during the second half of the game in the World of Ruin, the entire party-gathering mechanic hinges on world exploration if you want to re-recruit your full roster.

One of the most common praises directed at Lufia II centers on its brilliant dungeon design. The game features unique puzzle gimmicks and exploration mechanics reminiscent of The Legend of Zelda, utilizing specialized tools to solve environmental hazards. Every dungeon comes packed with secret rooms containing rare loot. It even carries a touch of Metroidvania flair, as tools acquired in later dungeons can be taken back to earlier areas to uncover hidden rewards.

Dragon Eggs are also scattered across the world and yield stat-enhancing potions, rare fruits, or powerful rings once you deliver them. Most importantly, Lufia II features Capsule Monsters that battle alongside your party. They act independently and evolve by eating items from your inventory, meaning you need a vast hoard of gear to feed your companions. Raising them isn't about grinding levels but about exploring dungeons, accumulating gold, buying items, and tracking down rare drops.

If a JRPG strikes a clean balance between combat progression and world exploration, it is Super Mario RPG. Mindless grinding is rarely necessary. And while leveling up offers significant stat boosts, grinding levels can feel overly tedious. True to its Mario roots, the game packs plenty of environmental puzzles and secrets that make exploration well worth your jumping time.

For example, expanding your maximum Flower Points requires hunting down specific hidden items across the world. You can also hunt for Frog Coins, a rare currency you can trade for powerful accessories and items. Game-changing equipment also hides off the beaten path, such as the Lazy Shell, which bestows absurd defensive stats that practically trivialize combat. It's a prime example of how vital exploration can be in shaping your party's strength.

Among the endless stream of compliments Chrono Trigger has received over the years, praise for its pacing remains one of the most common. Chrono Trigger omitted random encounters entirely, an unheard-of design choice for its era. Furthermore, the game never demanded grinding, letting you push through main-story challenges smoothly without ever looking back. That holds true right up until you acquire the Epoch.

Once you gain access to the Epoch, you can travel between eras seamlessly to visit hidden islands packed with optional dungeons and formidable bosses. These optional challenges present a far steeper curve than the main narrative, but with proper preparation and strategy, they are completely manageable. These side areas contain the absolute best endgame equipment while offering deep narrative payoffs for the party's backstories. On top of that, the game features numerous alternate endings accessible via New Game+, letting you confront Lavos at different points in time and proving that Chrono Trigger's spirit of exploration extends directly into its narrative structure.

These SNES JRPGs have stories Where the World Feels Like It’s Slowly Falling Apart Around the Player.

Share your take with fellow players. Keep it constructive, relevant, and respectful.

Which Airlines Fly To The Most Destinations? Some May Surprise You

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Recently, I wrote a post about the airlines that fly to the most countries. Turkish Airlines wins that title by a long shot, and has a 30+ country lead over the second most “global” airline.

In this post, I want to address a different but related topic — which airlines serve the most destinations? As you’d expect, this is a very different list. That’s because serving a lot of countries is much easier if you’re an airline that has a lot of service in Europe and Africa, given the number of countries there that are in close proximity. It’s one of the reason that US airlines don’t score that well when it comes to absolute number of countries served, despite otherwise being among the biggest carriers in the world.

However, the list of total number of destinations served is a different story…

I don’t think anyone will be surprised by the three airlines that serve the most destinations. As it currently stands:

This makes sense. In terms of fleets, the “big three” US carriers are among the biggest in the world. A majority of their networks are domestic, which is why the airlines don’t rank that high when it comes to total number of countries served, but they do great when it comes to the total number of destinations served. Note that the number of destinations also includes flights operated by their regional subsidiaries.

I don’t think the order above will surprise anyone. United is of course the most global US airline, and has a very impressive route network, including with ultra long haul flights. Meanwhile American is a massive domestic airline, and has a strong network in Latin America and the Caribbean, which is why it doesn’t appear that far behind United in terms of international destinations. And Delta, ummm, gets a revenue premium.

Just in the interest of being thorough, I’m sure some people are curious how many destinations Southwest serves, since it’s an absolutely massive airline in the United States, though doesn’t operate long haul flights. That number is less impressive — it’s a total of 120 destinations, including 104 domestic destinations and 16 international destinations. That’s because the airline is more about depth of service than breadth of service, and doesn’t have a regional subsidiary.

After US carriers, which airlines otherwise top the list of most destinations served? Most people wouldn’t be surprised to learn that Turkish takes that spot, serving 269 destinations, including 46 domestic destinations and 223 international destinations.

What about after that? Here are the next eight airlines (I think this is where some of the airlines may surprise people):

It’s interesting just how different the airlines on the list are. You have the “big three” Chinese carriers, the “big three” European carriers, and then you have Ryanair and Air Canada.

When you put it all together, below is the chart with the 12 airlines serving the most total destinations.

In the interest of being thorough, for those curious about Gulf carriers, Qatar Airways is in first place among them, serving 175 destinations, so it doesn’t quite make the list. Meanwhile Ethiopian Airlines, as Africa’s most global airline, serves 163 destinations

While Turkish Airlines flies to the most countries of any airline in the world, it doesn’t serve the most destinations. Instead that title goes to United, with the “big three” US carriers taking the three top spots. Then Turkish is in fourth place, and is followed by the “big three” Chinese carriers, the “big three” European carriers, and then also Ryanair and Air Canada.

Are you surprised by any of the airlines serving the most destinations?

Tested: Electric Genesis GV60 Magma Is Louder Than a Corvette Stingray

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Enable the Virtual Gear Shift mode’s simulated eight-speed DCT and 9000-RPM V-6, and the GV60 Magma howls like a beast.

Welcome to Car and Driver's Testing Hub, where we zoom in on the test numbers. We've been pushing vehicles to their limits since 1956 to provide objective data to bolster our subjective impressions (you can see how we test here).

The Genesis GV60 Magma is, well, red-hot. With up to 641 horsepower under your right foot, the electric crossover is a performance machine. Plus, as we detailed in a recent story, the performance GV60 doesn’t sacrifice any of that performance while in its faux-shifting mode, unlike the mechanically identical Hyundai Ioniq 5 N. In our testing, we found that not only is the GV60 Magma a performance monster, but it shouts like one, too.

See all results for new 2027 Genesis GV60 Magma for sale near 60091

At full throttle and set to its Virtual Shift Mode, which simulates an eight-speed dual-clutch automatic transmission and a 9000-rpm V-6, we recorded the Magma’s interior volume at 102 decibels. That’s considerably louder than the Ioniq 5 N’s 71-dBA max. It’s also louder than a 2027 Chevrolet Corvette Stingray with its 535-hp 6.7-liter V-8. Even when equipped with its optional $5395 quad center-exit exhaust system, the Vette reached a peak of 97 dBA at full chat when we activated its Track mode.

Sure, the Magma’s volume stems from a few lines of code and some speakers masquerading as an engine, while the Corvette has the real thing. But the Magma’s performance is very real, and despite being fake, its simulated shifting and adjustable engine noise are impressive facsimiles.

Jack Fitzgerald’s love for cars stems from his as yet unshakable addiction to Formula 1. After a brief stint as a detailer for a local dealership group in college, he knew he needed a more permanent way to drive all the new cars he couldn’t afford and decided to pursue a career in auto writing. By hounding his college professors at the University of Wisconsin-Milwaukee, he was able to travel Wisconsin seeking out stories in the auto world before landing his dream job at Car and Driver. His new goal is to delay the inevitable demise of his 2010 Volkswagen Golf.

iOS Startup Performance in Practice, Part 2: Measuring App Launch Time in Production

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

How to measure startup on real user devices, define trustworthy production percentiles, and use XCTest and Instruments to explain regressions.

The easiest way to measure app launch time is to start a stopwatch, open the app, and write down the number.

It is also one of the easiest ways to produce a measurement that cannot be reproduced, compared, or trusted.

An iOS launch depends on more than the code in the repository. The result changes with the process state, system caches, device model, thermal state, available memory, network behavior, stored user data, app version, and the destination the user is trying to open. Two measurements can both be technically correct while describing different experiences.

In Part 1, we separated cold launch, warm launch, resume, scene creation, and entry points. We also defined three possible endpoints: First Draw, Meaningful Content, and Time to Interactive.

Now we can turn that model into a measurement system.

This article is the second part of iOS Startup Performance in Practice, a six-part series:

The goal of this part is not to find a single perfect number. It is to build a production measurement system that describes what real users experience - and then use controlled tests and profiling to explain why that distribution changes.

Suppose one engineer reports a launch time of 1.4 seconds and another reports 2.1 seconds. Before comparing them, we need to know:

Without these answers, the numbers are labels rather than evidence.

Let’s start with production metrics. For every launch metric, define a measurement contract:

Real user launches of app version `8.24.0`

New-process launch; resume reported separately

Icon, link, notification, and quick action segmented where custom telemetry allows it

System launch start for MetricKit; first application marker for custom metrics

First Draw for the system metric; Home Interactive for the product metric

Signed-out and authenticated users reported separately

Device performance tier, iOS version, app version, and migration state

Seven complete days after the target rollout reaches a stable cohort

MetricKit or Organizer for system launch; custom telemetry for product milestones

Internal builds, synthetic runs, invalid or incomplete milestone sequences

This table describes measurement on user devices. There are no fixed test phones, stubbed networks, or XCTest iterations in the production contract. Real device and network diversity are not noise to remove; they are part of the experience we are trying to understand.

The first rule of launch measurement is therefore:

Define the population and user-visible endpoint before naming the number.

Instead of writing “launch time is 1.8 seconds,” write:

For production new-process launches of version 8.24.x, Time to First Draw is 1.1 seconds at p50 and 1.8 seconds at p90 across 2.4 million samples. Authenticated users on the lowest device tier have a p90 of 2.6 seconds.

The values are illustrative, but the form is important: it tells us who experienced the delay, which boundary was measured, and whether the percentile is statistically meaningful.

There is a fundamental limitation in iOS startup measurement:

This means one production number cannot answer every startup question.

Use Xcode Organizer or MetricKit to understand the system-observed launch distribution. These sources can describe Time to First Draw, optimized launch behavior influenced by prewarming, resume duration, and extended launch measurements supported by the OS version.

This is the authoritative source for questions such as:

Use lightweight custom telemetry for milestones the operating system cannot infer:

This is the authoritative source for questions such as:

Do not present an application timer that begins in didFinishLaunching as full launch time. Name it by its actual boundaries, for example AppCodeToHomeInteractive or RouteReceivedToDestinationInteractive.

Together, the two clocks give us a useful production model:

System launch distribution and device/OS trends

Entry-point-specific Meaningful Content and TTI

Instruments, XCTest, Xcode Organizer, MetricKit, and custom telemetry do not compete with one another. They answer different questions.

What startup time do real users experience?

Organizer, MetricKit, and production milestone telemetry

How does the distribution change by app version, device, OS, or user state?

Production telemetry segmented by those dimensions

How long until a requested destination becomes useful?

Which application service delays startup?

Separate resume metric and lifecycle instrumentation

A common mistake is to use the most convenient tool and then reformulate the problem so that it aligns with the result obtained using that tool.

How long does this scenario take under defined conditions?

These activities form a loop, but they should not be confused:

Instruments collects thread states, samples, stack traces, allocations, signposts, and launch phases. That visibility has a cost. Apple explicitly demonstrates that CPU time and wall-clock time in an instrumented trace can differ because profiling itself introduces overhead. It makes Instruments a diagnostic tool rather than the final stopwatch.

Use production data to discover and quantify the problem. Use Instruments to explain its root causes. Use XCTest to make the reproduction scenario repeatable and protect the issue from returning. Then, refer back to production data to verify the result.

As soon as production data reveals which cohort or user journey is performing slowly, create a benchmark that reproduces the situation. This serves as a tool for diagnosing and preventing regressions, rather than as a primary description of the user experience.

Launch tests are sensitive to the state of the whole device. A useful benchmark removes unrelated variance while preserving the characteristic that made the production cohort slow: device class, data volume, migration state, authentication path, or entry point.

The simulator is useful for development, lifecycle experiments, and fast feedback. It is not a reliable representation of iPhone launch performance because it runs on Mac hardware, shares host resources, and does not reproduce the same storage, memory, thermal, scheduling, and rendering environment.

Use physical devices for performance conclusions.

The minimum useful device set normally includes:

Do not compare an “after” result from a new phone with a “before” result from an old one.

Measure a Release configuration, or a dedicated Performance configuration derived from Release.

Avoid drawing conclusions from a Debug build. Debugger attachment, assertions, sanitizers, logging, and the absence of compiler optimizations can change both the duration and the shape of launch.

Keep observability enabled, but make it lightweight. Signposts are designed for this purpose; verbose synchronous logging is not.

Do not cool the device only for the “after” measurement. The goal is not to produce the smallest possible number. The goal is to make before and after comparable.

Startup often depends more on user state than teams initially expect.

The fixture should be deterministic. A performance test that uses an account whose data changes every day is not deterministic even if the test code never changes.

The network is part of the user experience, but it is usually a poor dependency for a code-regression benchmark. Latency, DNS, TLS, server load, routing, and radio state can overwhelm a small application-side change.

For local and CI measurements, prefer one of these contracts:

Then validate real network behavior separately in production.

There is no public API that reliably clears every relevant system cache and requests a “true cold launch.” Force-quitting an app and immediately reopening it creates a new process, but the second launch can still benefit from warmed pages and system services.

Apple recommends warm launches for repeatable development measurements because they are more consistent. XCTest also performs a throwaway iteration before recording measured iterations, reducing first-run cache variance.

That does not mean cold launch is unimportant. It means it needs a different strategy.

Use warm launches for high-signal regression detection:

Evaluate cold behavior through a combination of:

Never label “terminate and immediately relaunch five times” as five cold launches. It is five new-process launches under increasingly warm system conditions.

XCTApplicationLaunchMetric provides the most practical automated baseline for application launch.

A minimal UI performance test looks like this:

Without an extended launch task, XCTApplicationLaunchMetric() measures to the first frame. XCTest runs the measured block repeatedly and ignores an additional initial iteration used to reduce warm-up variance.

If the requirement is that the first frame is also responsive to input, use:

This waits until the main thread is responsive after the first frame. It is often a better default for products that can technically draw a frame while still blocking user interaction.

The two metrics answer different questions:

First frame, plus any explicitly registered extended launch tasks

XCTApplicationLaunchMetric(waitUntilResponsive: true)

Do not silently switch between them when comparing builds.

Launch arguments and environment variables are useful for choosing deterministic fixtures, but the application must process them carefully.

Do not replace the normal startup architecture with a completely different test-only app. The test should control external variance, not remove the code you intend to measure.

A production application usually needs several launch tests:

Entry-point-specific journeys can be added separately, but remember that the built-in application launch metric ends at a system launch milestone. If the product destination becomes useful later, add an application-owned measurement for that interval.

Xcode performance tests can compare results with an accepted baseline and flag regressions. A baseline is only meaningful for a stable environment.

Do not share one absolute baseline across:

For CI, record the device identity, OS, Xcode version, commit, build configuration, fixture, and raw measurements with every run. If any of those dimensions changes, treat the comparison as a new series until you have evidence that the values remain comparable.

Once the benchmark shows that launch is slow, open the same scenario with the App Launch template in Instruments.

Profile a Release build on the same class of physical device used for measurement.

The App Launch template separates major phases such as:

In the trace, inspect more than the total duration:

Pin the app launch phases, main thread, and relevant worker threads. Add Points of Interest so that application-owned phases appear on the same timeline.

Then form one narrow hypothesis. For example:

The root interface waits synchronously for the complete catalog cache, although the first screen needs only the first page.

Change that behavior, return to the controlled XCTest, and measure again. Do not report the duration of the new Instruments trace as the improvement; report the repeated benchmark under the original contract.

System metrics can tell us when the first frame appears. They cannot infer that a product screen contains meaningful data or that a requested route is ready.

Use OSSignposter to make application phases visible:

A small interval wrapper can keep ownership explicit:

The end marker must represent a real product condition. For a home screen, that may require:

Do not end the interval because a convenient callback fired.

XCTest can record the duration between matching signpost events:

This creates an application-specific benchmark, but it has an important boundary: the signpost cannot begin before application code runs. It measures the application-owned interval, not the full system launch.

Good startup signposts are few, stable, and architectural:

Avoid adding an interval around every function. A trace with hundreds of unstable names becomes harder to compare and expensive to maintain.

Signpost names also become part of the observability contract. Rename them deliberately.

An app can produce a fast first frame and still feel slow.

The system First Draw metric may report approximately one second. The user asked for a product and could not act on it for three and a half seconds.

The problem begins when the first number is presented as the complete user experience.

Define a product milestone for each important entry point:

Requested product visible and primary action enabled

Camera preview active and accepting input

This is also why a universal “startup budget” is insufficient. The system launch budget can be shared, but route execution and destination readiness need their own budgets.

Production launch performance is a distribution across users, devices, operating systems, application states, and entry points. A single average hides the experiences that most need attention.

For production launch data, report at least:

Always segment the distribution before interpreting it. At minimum, compare:

A global p90 can remain unchanged while the p90 on the oldest supported devices regresses by several seconds. It can also appear to improve simply because adoption shifted toward newer devices. Segmentation is part of the metric, not an optional dashboard filter.

Do not use production maximum as a release KPI. In a sufficiently large population, isolated extreme values are expected and may reflect interruptions the application cannot control. Use high percentiles, SLO compliance, and sample count to quantify the tail; use diagnostics to investigate pathological individual cases.

For a controlled test, preserve every raw iteration and report the median, p90, and maximum. A small local dataset answers a different question: whether the scenario is stable enough to reproduce a change.

Suppose ten controlled runs produce these durations in seconds:

The median describes the center, while the p90 and maximum reveal instability that an average would blur. Do not delete the slow runs merely because they are inconvenient. Investigate whether they came from the application or from a documented failure of the experiment.

Ten local runs can provide quick feedback, but they do not make a production-quality p99. High percentiles require many samples.

Do not compare a p90 from ten local launches with a p90 from millions of production launches as though they were the same experiment.

Production data is the primary description of startup quality. Controlled tests answer whether a code change affected a reproducible scenario, but they cannot represent every real user.

Xcode Organizer aggregates launch performance from users who share diagnostics and allows teams to compare distributions across app versions and devices. It is the fastest way to establish a system-level production baseline before building any custom pipeline.

MetricKit reports include histogrammed launch and resume information rather than one application-wide scalar. Depending on the OS and SDK generation, launch data includes time-to-first-draw, optimized time-to-first-draw for prewarmed launches, resume time, and extended launch measurements.

Apple is evolving MetricKit toward the newer MetricManager, MetricReport, and typed MetricResult APIs in upcoming SDKs. If you adopt those APIs, isolate the SDK-specific ingestion layer. The measurement model should remain stable even when the transport type changes:

Do not upload a daily payload and discard its dimensions. At minimum, preserve:

Aggregating everything into one average makes regressions on older devices almost invisible.

System metrics understand launch and rendering. They do not automatically know that the product card requested by a notification is ready for purchase.

For entry-point-specific Meaningful Content and Time to Interactive, add privacy-safe custom telemetry:

Avoid including raw URLs, notification payloads, user identifiers, search text, or product data unless the analytics design explicitly requires and permits them. The useful dimensions are normally categories, not content.

Startup observability runs on the critical path, so its design matters.

A lightweight custom recorder might look like this:

Even this code starts only after application code exists. It must never be described as measuring the full interval from the user tap.

The following protocol is a useful starting point for a production team. It begins with user devices and introduces laboratory measurements only after the production metric identifies a problem.

Select the launch scenarios that represent meaningful user traffic and risk:

Keep launch and resume in separate series.

Before creating test fixtures, define the production segments needed to explain the journey: app version, device tier, OS, authentication state, entry-point category, and migration state. Do not collect dimensions that have no analytical purpose.

Use Organizer or MetricKit for system launch and resume distributions. Add lightweight custom milestones for Meaningful Content and Time to Interactive. Buffer and upload the data outside the launch critical path.

Collect a complete, stable rollout window. Report p50, p90, p95, sample count, SLO compliance, and the important segments. Treat resume as a separate metric.

Determine whether the regression belongs to a device tier, OS, app version, entry point, user state, or migration path. This cohort becomes the specification for the controlled experiment.

Choose stable physical devices based on the affected production population. Create deterministic fixtures that reproduce its data volume, authentication state, feature flags, cache state, and network contract.

Use XCTApplicationLaunchMetric for First Draw or responsiveness and signposts for application-owned stages. Use Instruments to connect the slow interval to threads, stacks, dependencies, and launch phases.

Measure after every meaningful change. Once the improvement is reproducible, run the benchmark regularly on fixed hardware and alert on a sustained regression or budget violation rather than one noisy sample.

Compare Organizer, MetricKit, and custom product milestones by app version and the original affected segment. A local win is complete only when the relevant production distribution moves in the expected direction.

A production report should first show the user impact and the affected cohort, then connect it to the controlled experiment used to explain the change:

The exact values above are illustrative. The important property is traceability: the report begins with the real-user regression, identifies the cohort, shows how it was reproduced, and defines how the fix will be validated.

Faster for which scenario, device, endpoint, and percentile?

At this point, the pieces form one system:

Each layer corrects a blind spot in another:

The goal is not to choose one source of truth. It is to know which source is authoritative for each question.

Correct launch measurement begins with real users.

First define the production population, lifecycle path, entry point, start boundary, and meaningful endpoint. Collect enough samples to report p50, p90, p95, SLO compliance, and the device, OS, app-version, and user-state segments that can explain the distribution.

Use Organizer and MetricKit for system-observed production launch and resume metrics. Add privacy-safe custom telemetry for route resolution, Meaningful Content, and Time to Interactive. When a cohort regresses, reproduce that cohort on physical hardware, use XCTest to make the scenario repeatable, and use Instruments to understand the phases, threads, and stacks behind it. Finally, return to the same production segment to validate the fix.

A benchmark tells you whether the code changed. Production telemetry tells you whether the user experience changed.

In Part 3: Finding Bottlenecks from dyld to the First Frame, we will use the measurement system built here to read an App Launch trace phase by phase. We will examine pre-main work, linked frameworks, static initialization, disk I/O, main-thread blocking, rendering, and the difference between CPU time and waiting time.

Wandrd’s Magnetic Camera Straps Are My New Favorite Accessory

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

More than a decade ago, Peak Design rethought the humble camera strap. This was a necessary development. When you buy a camera, manufacturers almost always toss in a chintzy, uninspired strap with their logo on it. These cheap straps have cheap mounting systems that make it hard to quickly remove the strap from the camera when you need to, like when you're attaching your camera to a tripod or gimbal and you’d rather not have a dangling strap whacking you in the face.

Peak Design solved this with its Anchor Links system, small circular nubs you leave attached to camera eyelets that make detaching or even swapping straps easy. The quick-connect mechanism is now the de facto industry standard and has inspired countless copycats.

Now, Wandrd has outdone that design using magnets.

“We're photographers ourselves, so we're constantly ideating, tinkering, thinking of ways that things can be better,” Ryan Cope, Wandrd cofounder, tells WIRED. “A camera strap like this has been at the back of our minds for a while.”

Wandrd—known for its camera bags—launched its Magnetic Camera Strap system on Thursday. It features a circular anchor similar to Peak Design, called “Torus Links.” The anchors attach to your camera’s eyelets and are rated to carry up to 198 pounds—so unless you're bench-pressing your camera, they'll do the job. If the cord does start to wear down over time, you'll first see yellow under the black fabric—a sign you should replace the Torus Link. If it's red, you should stop using the link immediately—a warning system borrowed from Peak Design. (Wandrd offers a two-year warranty.)

Unlike Peak Design's anchors, the Wandrd anchors look like donuts, with a hole in the middle. When you attach the strap, the embedded magnets guide the Torus Link into the strap's CoreLock mechanism, which pinches into the hole to secure the camera body. The magnets make connecting a strap easier, and they keep the strap fastened to the anchor, but you can’t just pull the strap off like you’re pulling a MagSafe accessory on your phone. You’ll need to use the strap’s dual-pinch mechanism to remove it from the Torus Link, a design that’s more secure.

The Torus Link is the donut-shaped object that attaches to your camera. On the right is the Magnetic CoreLock at the end of the strap. Pinch the top and bottom of this mechanism and let the magnets guide the strap into the Torus Link.

It’s a much faster way to connect and disconnect a strap, and that speed pays off in the field. This week, I had to photograph a product during a press briefing. Usually, I use Peak Design's Leash and, instead of taking off the strap, I bunch it up in my hand when it's not around my neck. It's not that it's hard to remove—it's just an extra step I have to think about. But Wandrd's magnetic system felt faster to unlock mid-shoot when I wanted to lower the camera for an awkward, low-angle shot, and reattaching the strap was practically instantaneous. I did the whole swap one-handed, with the magnets doing most of the work.

“I love magnets, man,” Cope says. “I love the simplicity they create—guiding things into place. You don't always have a place to put your camera down; I like that magnets help guide them and are a lot more secure, and they're also just fun.”

I have two Torus Links on my camera body, one on the right eyelet and another on the tripod baseplate. But my favorite configuration turns the strap into a single-point sling. The strap has a built-in “Torus Loop” on the buckle—clip one end directly into it to create a closed loop worn across your torso, leaving just a single magnetic connector to snap onto your camera. When you unclip the camera to shoot freely, the strap stays neatly in place across your body instead of sliding off your shoulder.

The nylon felt comfortable across my chest and near my neck—there's no padding, but that's intentional. Cope says Wandrd spent two years developing this custom “liquid nylon” tubular webbing that is softer along the edges, so it won't feel like the strap is cutting into your neck after a long day of wear. The lack of metal throughout the strap system also prevents the strap from scratching up your camera gear.

Best of all, you don't have to replace your strap to use Wandrd's system. If you have a strap you love—maybe a fancy leather strap someone gifted you—Wandrd is selling a DIY kit ($29) that lets you attach its Magnetic CoreLock system to the ends of your preferred strap. Otherwise, the strap kit comes in a 30-mm size ($69), a 15-mm “Lite" ($49), and a wrist strap ($39).

I don't know if Wandrd's system will see the same success as Peak Design's Anchor Links, but I don't see myself switching back anytime soon. Magnets are just too dang cool.

Skip the Glacier Crowds for Waterton Lakes National Park Right Across the Border

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Crypt Lake Hike - stock photo Crypt Lake hike in Waterton Lakes National Park (Photo: Getty)

A weak summer sun strains to break through a darkening wall of clouds, its rays catching the shale. My boots methodically kick the rock loose as I work my way up a steep slope of scree. I’m slick with sweat, my helmet is chafing, and I’m completely alone in my misery. But the painstaking plod up the ridgeline of Bertha Peak in Waterton Lakes National Park does come with its rewards. When I stop to catch my breath, my gaze sweeps south toward the rugged peaks of neighboring Glacier National Park before flicking north toward my home in Calgary, Canada, and the wild swathes of prairie that define southern Alberta.

Named for Bertha Ekelund, a bold, “wayward” Waterton pioneer whose colorful past included attempts to pass counterfeit money, Bertha Peak reflects something of the untamed wilderness the local legend called home. As I take in the view, it strikes me that this is a landscape that still rewards those willing to venture a little farther—something I’ve striven to do since my first visit to this park as a child.

While hikers flock to Glacier’s trails just across the border, I’m happy to enjoy the same wild landscapes without the crowds. And, at a time when Canada-U.S. relations are increasingly defined by political tension and division, this shared wilderness offers a different perspective. Established in 1932 as the world’s first International Peace Park, Waterton-Glacier reminds us that borders can connect as much as they divide. Peace parks have now been established in 30 places around the world, serving as symbols of cooperation, conservation, and understanding between neighboring nations. For Canadians like me and in-the-know Americans, Waterton offers a quieter way to experience the Rockies, with room to roam and the chance to find solitude in the mountains.

Despite sharing a border with Glacier, the experience of the two parks couldn’t be more different. Waterton Lakes receives a fraction of Glacier’s visitors; in 2025, Glacier saw more than 3 million visitors compared with just half a million in Waterton. You can drive the scenic Red Rock Parkway to the ruddy landscape of Red Rock Canyon without waiting in line, worrying about closures due to congestion, or having to book a shuttle. And while backcountry campsites still require permits, you don’t have to fight the crowds when reservations open to secure a night in the wilderness. A variety of trails from the family-friendly hike to Bertha Falls to the challenging Carthew-Alderson Trail, along with non-hiking pursuits such as kayaking, cycling, and dark-sky viewing, make Waterton an easy addition to a trip across the border.

In 2027, Waterton will also mark the tenth anniversary of the Kenow Wildfire, which burned nearly 20,000 hectares of Waterton in 2017, and its effects are still visible. Where thick stands of trees once stood, charred trunks now rise from lush regrowth, surrounded by the vibrant violet blooms of fireweed. The anniversary provides an opportunity to explore a larger story of shared landscapes, resilience, and cross-border conservation. As governments debate borders, the mountains offer a clear reminder: here, the international boundary is little more than a line on a map, and hiking across it feels like an uncomplicated expression of peace. Even amid the devastation, the fire revealed the resilience of the ecosystem and the importance of giving it space to recover.

A decade later, visitors can see forests regenerating, wildflowers returning and wildlife—including the broad-shouldered grizzlies, shaggy plains bison, and the elusive wolverine—moving through a landscape reshaped by fire. It’s a reminder that conservation doesn’t stop at the border: Waterton and Glacier remain a rare model of international cooperation, where hikers cross between countries through the mountains, wildlife moves freely, and conservation efforts extend across two nations.

The Prince of Wales Hotel, one of Canada’s most famous heritage sites, was built in part to lure booze-starved Americans across the border and away from the restrictions of Prohibition. You know you’ve arrived in Waterton Lakes when the hotel, perched on a grassy knoll above Upper Waterton Lake, comes into view against the mountainous backdrop. With the park’s striking peaks and indigo lakes, the hotel faces stiff competition for the spotlight. Yet the building has become an icon in its own right, symbolizing the enduring character of this under-the-radar corner of the Canadian Rockies. Turning 100 in 2027, the hotel was the brainchild of Louis Hill, a driving force behind the development of Glacier National Park. Hill saw Americans’ frequent trips to Europe as a failure of domestic tourism.

World War I and Prohibition delayed construction, and the hotel finally opened in 1927. When Alberta repealed its prohibition laws in 1924, Waterton gained a new appeal for Americans seeking a legal drink. Nowadays, visitors can take in panoramic views of Upper Waterton Lake and the surrounding mountains while enjoying a meal or drink in the historic lounge before retiring to a room that retains its original charm. The lakeview rooms are well worth the splurge (a night at the height of summer will set you back about $600-$700 CAD / $425-$495 USD). The Royal High Tea is a guest-favorite; a chance to get dressed up and savor the hotel’s old-world charm alongside delicate treats and a steaming pot of tea.

Today the hotel also draws visitors for the wilderness on its doorstep, and trust me, Waterton’s trails will make you earn the view. If you have time for only one adventure, I recommend Crypt Lake, the park’s best-known hike, which begins with a boat ride—expect to pay $35 CAD/$25 USD for a ticket—across Upper Waterton Lake before climbing toward a turquoise lake hemmed in by towering peaks. Near the top, a narrow ledge, a 60-foot ladder, and a short tunnel through the mountain turn a demanding hike into a full-on expedition.

Elsewhere, the landscape changes character. Red Rock Canyon glows crimson along the Red Rock Parkway, its rust-colored stone an unexpected contrast to the surrounding mountains. At Bertha Falls, water spills down a smooth rock face before the trail continues toward Bertha Lake, a high alpine basin that feels a world away from the townsite below. And from Bear’s Hump, the Prince of Wales Hotel looks almost miniature against the vast sweep of mountains and water. These are my go-to family-friendly hikes in Waterton, offering big scenery and memorable journeys without the commitment of a full day on the trail.

For those seeking a longer hike, I also highly recommend Akamina Ridge, where a challenging scramble along an exposed ridgeline leads to sweeping views of Wall Lake, Forum Lake and the rugged peaks straddling the Alberta-British Columbia border. Another favorite of mine is the long Carthew-Alderson traverse, which reveals a wilder side of the park, where the crowds thin and the scale of the landscape takes over.

From the shore of Upper Waterton Upper Lake, the international border is invisible, but out on the water, you realize how arbitrary it really is—mountains, forests, and wildlife pay no mind to the line dividing Alberta from Montana. Boarding the Waterton Shoreline Cruise Co., the world’s first International Peace Park feels like a living reality as the same continuous wilderness stretches across two countries.

Opt for the longer interpretive cruise to Goat Haunt, Montana, which includes a 30-minute stop at the ranger station. You’ll take in sweeping peak views, spot wildlife, and enjoy the novelty of being in two countries in one afternoon without passing a border checkpoint.

Hikers can cross the international border between Waterton and Glacier National Park, but must carry a valid passport and follow customs reporting requirements on both sides. Those entering the U.S. must report through the CBP ROAM app on the day of crossing, while anyone returning to Canada must report their re-entry to the Canada Border Services Agency (CBSA) by phone. A popular option is to hike one-way to Goat Haunt and return by boat.

Waterton’s history isn’t confined to its mountain trails or the old buildings of the townsite either. Believe it or not, you can scuba dive in Waterton’s lakes. The water may be cold, but visibility can be excellent, making the sheltered and scenic waters of Emerald Bay of Upper Waterton Lake particularly appealing to divers. Beneath the surface, at a depth of about 65 feet, lies the wreck of the Gertrude, an early-20th-century paddle wheeler.

If diving in chilly mountain water isn’t your idea of fun, there are plenty of warmer-weather ways to get on the lakes. I recommend renting a kayak, canoe or stand-up paddleboard and getting out on the surface of the water to soak in those mountain views from a different perspective. Parks Canada does require non-motorized watercraft entering Waterton’s lakes to be rented within the park, with options including Blakiston & Company in town and Cameron Lake Boat Rentals on the shore of Cameron Lake.

For a glimpse of Waterton after dark, join a stargazing tour with Dark Sky Guides, co-founded by Keith Robinson, who has been guiding professionally for 25 years and was born and raised in the park. Waterton became an official International Dark Sky Park in 2021, and Parks Canada replaced the community’s streetlights with custom amber fixtures designed to reduce light pollution, using roughly one-third the power of lighting in major cities. The result is an unusually dark sky, making Waterton one of the most southerly places where I have consistently seen the dancing northern lights. The tour offers an introduction to the night sky while also exploring Waterton’s nocturnal wildlife—an activity I’ve found that’s great to do with kids.

If you’ve seen the hit show The Last of Us, you may remember a winter scene that gave you chills just watching it. That’s because winter in Waterton, where part of the show was filmed, is not for the faint of heart. However, I’ve found that if I’m willing to brave wild winds, freezing temperatures, and heavy snow I have the frost-dusted landscape almost entirely to myself.

Winter is also prime time for stargazing and aurora spotting, and with the earlier sunsets, I appreciate that I don’t have to stay up until the wee hours to catch them. By day, the park becomes a snowy playground, with cross-country skiing, snowshoeing, fat biking, wildlife watching, and winter walks. The best thing to do in winter here is to ski or snowshoe approximately two miles toward Cameron Lake. I book a couple of spots in a nearby winter-only six-person backcountry cabin (via the Alpine Club of Canada) and enjoy a family-friendly backcountry experience without avalanche risk. I also love to explore the Red Rock Parkway, which closes to vehicles in winter and becomes a nine-mile route for winter recreation.

While most businesses close for the winter, the charming Kilmory Lodge remains open. In the evenings, I cuddle up beside the roaring fire with a piping hot drink, then settle in for a meal at the Lamp Post Dining Room. The Parks Canada visitor center is also open daily during the snowy season, providing maps, trail and weather information, and winter safety advice.

One important note: there are no grocery stores or gas stations operating in Waterton during winter, so arrive with a full tank and provisions.

Jennifer Malloy is a freelance writer based in Calgary, Alberta. Her recent work highlights the best runcations around the world.
