Paramount’s 4-Part Action Fantasy Epic Has One of the Best Seasons in TV History

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

With one of the best villains of all time

Few things feel more grown-up than dealing with the consequences of your actions. Whether you stay up too late and still have to get up the next day or make a mistake at work and have to answer for it, adulthood means no one’s coming to fix things. For the most part, it’s all up to you.

The Legend of Korra has been the subject of much criticism over the years, but it’s always been a show about its young hero Korra (Janet Varney) growing from a feisty teen into a relatively less feisty adult. It’s not until season 3 that she experiences her biggest change, dealing with the consequences of one of her biggest decisions: leaving the spirit world portals open.

It’s one of the best decisions Korra makes, but it also creates her most thought-provoking villain.

Season 3 is appropriately titled “Book 3: Change,” and it picks up after the Harmonic Convergence, the planetary alignment that flooded the world with spiritual energy. After a great battle (literally, Korra becomes a kaiju via astral projection), Korra leaves the portals open, believing that people and the spirits should live in harmony together. It’s a decision with vast ramifications, from spirits adorably popping up throughout Republic City to the awakening of air-bending skills in random people, reviving a culture that had been nearly lost.

One of these new benders is Zaheer (Henry Rollins), a member of the Red Lotus, which is a secret anarchist organization whose mission is to disrupt world order and eliminate the Avatar cycle. Zaheer uses his airbending to escape his high-security prison and rescue his comrades, who are some of the coolest villains in animation. These include the lavabender Ghazan (Peter Giles), waterbender Ming Hua (Grey DeLisle), and combustion bender P’li (Kristy Wu).

Meanwhile, Korra and her team travel the world, slowly collecting new airbenders in the hopes of rebuilding the Air Nation.

“Change” is a blockbuster of a third season, taking full advantage of Studio Mir’s animation capabilities. It’s the one season the studio animated from start to finish, and it shows. While The Legend of Korra always had incredible fights, in this season, the bending takes center stage. Ming Hua offers a new flavor of waterbending; she doesn’t have arms, so she fashions several out of water, looking like a terrifying spider. P’Li’s combustion is accompanied by a high-pitched whine, with the energy launching like a rocket through her third eye. Her hair is braided, and she’s huge. On a design level, these are the types of characters you only need to see once to remember forever.

Increasing the sense of peril is the fact that the Red Lotus feels so adult and experienced when compared to Korra and her friends. Zaheer and P’Li are devoted partners. Together, these four are close comrades, with their experiences and crimes weathered on their skin. It results in some of the franchise’s best showdowns, often involving characters that were previously sidelined, like Tenzin (J.K. Simmons), who has a standout battle in the Air Temple, and Korra’s father Tonraq (James Remar), who comes to her aid and prevents her from being kidnapped by the Red Lotus.

Then there’s Zaheer himself. He is a blank slate, the quietest and least charming member of a gang of incredibly cool villains. He’s so detached from the world and so at home with his ideologies that he feels inaccessible — no one’s convincing this guy of anything. His one tether to the world is P’Li, a relationship that the season cashes in on in spectacular fashion.

In arguably one of the coldest moments in kids' animation, Zaheer kills the Earth Queen by taking the air out of her lungs. Her death throws Ba Sing Se into chaos. And while the series never backs away from his extremism, like every great villain, Zaheer makes a lot of sense. His beliefs cause Korra to wonder if maybe the world would be better off without corrupt leaders like the Earth Queen, who lives surrounded by obscene wealth and imprisons her subjects for her own gain while the people in her kingdom suffer.

When it comes to Korra, there’s so much breathing room when the show finally drops her love triangle with Mako (David Faustino) and Asami (Seychelle Gabriel). It’s a development that allows Korra to focus on other things, like how bad she is at talking to bureaucrats or her having to hold back when speaking with people that are incredibly annoying.

Revenge is a dish best served 12 years later

Korra gets to explore her dynamics with other characters, including Suyin (Anne Heche) and Lin Beifong (Mindy Sterling), two standouts in the franchise. She’s also frequently paired with Asami, with the two finally moving past the fact that they dated the same guy (even if they’ll end up falling in love with each other the following season).

“I think Korra was more likable in Book 3 because — but that was actually part of her arc that we’d set out to do — is that she was becoming more selfless, and it wasn’t just about her own stuff,” co-creator Bryan Konietzko told The Mary Sue in 2014.

By the end of the “Change,” Korra has saved the world once more, but is more depleted than ever. After being poisoned by the Red Lotus, she’s in a wheelchair, watching her past self’s civilization restored as the Air Nomads rise once more. Another adult lesson Korra learns? Sometimes life’s greatest moments are bittersweet.

The Legend of Korra is streaming on Paramount Plus

Add This Spicy Ingredient To Ground Beef For A Burger That's 10x Juicier

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

If you're looking for a burger patty that not only has a bit of kick, but also plenty of juice, you need 'nduja, a legendary Italian sausage. It is not only extremely flavorful, but also quite high in fat and moisture, making it a perfect addition no matter how you like to cook your burgers.

'Nduja is technically a type of salami, except it's far more decadent and incorporates a hefty dose of Calabrian chili peppers for next-level taste. Its high fat-to-lean meat ratio makes it perfect for introducing more moisture, even in small amounts. Even if you only have exceptionally lean meat on hand, one part 'nduja to four parts beef keeps your patties tender and juicy, and even helps develop a better crust.

The idea is similar to many meatball recipes from around the world that incorporate both pork and beef. Whereas beef provides body and a hearty texture, pork can provide extra fat, flavor, and moisture, working together to cook up something truly delicious. The peppers also create a slightly savory and spicy flavor profile that, while immediately noticeable, isn't so overwhelming that you might need to change up your preferred toppings. Each bite will drip with flavorful juices that mix with your condiments for the most moisture-rich burger possible.

Incorporating anything into a burger is a bit of a tightrope walk. You want to disperse your ingredients evenly but not work the muscle fibers so much that your patties end up dense or tough. 'Nduja's texture makes this quite easy, but you'll want to be careful about what type of meat you add it to.

Since it has a spreadable, pâté-like texture, working 'nduja directly into the meat is quite easy. Whether you mix it in thoroughly or squeeze pinches into already formed patties for pockets of flavor, you get plenty of fatty juices. Still, if you're worried about overworking your patties or want to add the salty meat at the very last minute, 'nduja is typically safe to eat right out of the package. Treat it like cheese and add a dollop after flipping your patties, letting it melt over the whole thing for a juicy entree.

If you want a custom burger blend, you have to be mindful of the proper fat-to-meat ratio. 'Nduja's exceptional fat content means it's best to add it to already lean blends so you don't end up with anything too greasy. The leaner your beef, the more 'nduja you can incorporate, so it's a balance between how much of its flavor you want included. Something that's at least 85% lean is ideal, letting you incorporate enough to taste without adding so much that you can't appreciate the beef's natural flavor.

Hutchinson beats Buatsi to win WBC light-heavyweight title

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Willy Hutchinson was crowned the WBC light-heavyweight champion after he produced the performance of his career to beat Joshua Buatsi in Manchester.

Hutchinson, 28, edged the tense rematch with the judges scoring it 117-112, 115-113, 115-113 to avenge his defeat by Buatsi in 2024.

Saturday's contest was initially for the interim title but was upgraded when American David Benavidez gave up the WBC gold to move up to cruiserweight.

Scotland's Hutchinson and Londoner Buatsi met for the second time, just over two years after the Londoner earned a split-decision win in a thrilling first contest, but this time it was Hutchinson who came out on top as he improved his record to 21 wins with two defeats.

It was a childhood dream come true for Hutchinson, who said before the fight he wrote about his dreams of becoming a WBC champion at the age of 13.

Buatsi looked a shadow of the fighter who put Hutchinson on the canvas twice in their first meeting, seeming short of ideas on how to catch the slick Scot.

Ticket sales were modest for a world title fight as it was clear that it did not capture the attention of the British public. But those inside made the atmosphere as raucous as possible, with the Manchester crowd favouring Hutchinson.

And they were delighted when the result was read out and Hutchinson became the eighth British light-heavyweight champion - the first since Nathan Cleverly in 2017.

Fight week was dominated by predictions that Buatsi, five years Hutchinson's senior, is over the hill.

And the Londoner did nothing to prove otherwise in Manchester as Hutchinson often toyed with his opponent in the centre of the ring.

The first fight in 2024 had Buatsi walking to the ring as a comfortable favourite, and he backed that up with crushing body shots to send Hutchinson tumbling twice.

But this time around, Hutchinson was a different fighter. In his own words, he had "matured" after picking up two wins since last facing Buatsi.

Hutchinson clearly had the crowd on his side as he made the walk to cheers, while Buatsi was met by a chorus of boos as he paraded around the ring before the bell.

For Buatsi, the strategy was very much the same as the first encounter - whip shots into the body - but Hutchinson now had answers.

The AO Arena is famous for its boxing nights with Manchester's favourite boxing son, Ricky Hatton, becoming world champion here for the first time in June 2005 in a thriller against Kostya Tszyu.

This did not reach those heights, as the upper tier was closed off because of a lack of ticket sales - and fans of Manchester's Jack Rafferty departed after he won the co-main event.

This did not deter Hutchinson from advancing in the latter rounds and landing rapid triple jabs, while also switching stances.

As Manchester royalty Bez, of the Happy Mondays, watched on from ringside, Hutchinson's corner told him "three rounds and you're a world champion" after the ninth.

And they were right. Despite Buatsi's advances late on, Hutchinson held out to become world champion for the first time.

Magnus Ditlev Sets New Iron-Distance World Best With 7:19:17 at Ironman Florida

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Magnus Ditlev set the fastest-ever Iron-distance time at the 2026 Ironman Florida (Photo by Sean M. Haffey/Getty Images for IRONMAN) (Photo: Getty Images for IRONMAN)

One month after a heartbreaking DNF in Kona, Magnus Ditlev (DEN) rode away from the field at Ironman Florida and never looked back. His 7:19:17 took 1:47 off the previous men’s iron-distance world best that Sam Laidlow (FRA) set at Challenge Roth earlier this year.

Florida was a chance at redemption for Ditlev, who had come agonizingly close to winning the Ironman World Championship in Kona. He’d taken the lead late on the bike and looked on course for the win until he abruptly came to a stop on the run with roughly 30 minutes of racing left.

However, ahead of Kona, Ditlev said he was putting up career-best numbers across the swim, bike, and run at his training camp in Colorado. In Florida, he finally got to reap the rewards of what fitness could produce all the way to the finish line.

Ditlev came out of the water in fifth, right on the heels of Ben Kanute (USA) and Rudy von Berg (USA). He took his time in T1 and started the bike nearly a minute down, but it didn’t take him long to ride back to the front. And then, just past mile 10, he made his move. No one could go with him, and they wouldn’t be able to stay remotely close to him either.

By the time Ditlev came off the bike, he was 11 minutes clear of a chase group that included Sam Long (USA), von Berg, and Kanute. His 3:51:16 was the fastest bike split ever recorded at an Ironman, nearly two minutes quicker than the 3:53:12 Robert Kallin (SWE) rode at Ironman Kalmar in August.

Off the bike, he once again took his time in transition. But transitions were the only slow part of Ditlev’s day. Once through T2, he picked up another minute on the chasers over the opening 10 miles. He hit halfway in 1:15, on pace for a 2:30 marathon and well within reach of Laidlow’s 7:21:04. With such an unassailable lead, one can assume he must’ve known the record was within reach to keep such an aggressive pace.

Ditlev eventually closed with a 2:33:31 marathon, crossing the line in 7:19:17. Von Berg took second, 14 minutes back, securing the early Kona slot and podium he’d come for. Matt Hanson (USA), meanwhile, ran a remarkable 2:28:07 to beat Long to the final podium spot, but still finished 16 minutes behind Ditlev.

Southwest Airlines Flight Attendant Allegedly Quit Job After Passenger-Facing Crew Dispute

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Journalist – Aaron is an aviation and travel journalist with 14 years of experience in the travel and tourism industry. His background spans airport operations, aviation infrastructure, and travel consulting, having worked with global organizations across New Zealand, Europe, and North America. Combining industry expertise with firsthand travel experience across more than 52 countries and five continents, Aaron covers airlines, airports, aviation policy, and the wider travel sector. He is based in Canterbury, New Zealand. His work has been published in Simple Flying and featured by MSN, SAM CHUI Aviation & Travel, EIN News, Pakistan Defence, and Climate Depot.

A Southwest Airlines flight attendant allegedly quit after a verbal argument with other cabin crew in front of a fully boarded plane. A report by View From The Wing has suggested that passengers were told a flight attendant was sick before the captain of the flight to Kansas City came on the loudspeaker and explained that the two had 'personality differences'.

Just this past Friday, Southwest flight 557 was set to operate from Dallas Love Field (DAL) to Kansas City International Airport (MCI), when the pushback of the jet was delayed due to the disagreement between the two co-workers. The argument set the flight back several hours.

Shared on a passenger's Reddit page, an eyewitness passenger onboard described the argument between the two Southwest flight attendants taking place at the front of the aircraft in full view for all of the passengers onboard. This resulted in the crew being taken off the aircraft and then it being blamed on them being sick.

This performance in front of the other passengers forced the captain to speak to the passengers onboard to explain the situation, with the blame being put down to personality differences. Further claims have suggested that the flight was already delayed by ten minutes, but the full delay of up to two hours before the jet finally pushed back and departed for Kansas City was down to the crew dispute.

Simple Flying reached out to Southwest for comment on Saturday; however, an airline spokesperson could not immediately be reached.

As suggested by VFTW, one of the Southwest flight attendants involved in the argument has since quit their job with the world's largest operator of the Boeing 737, after they were embarrassed after having a verbal argument with another crew member in front of a fully boarded aircraft.

Southwest flight 557, a regular scheduled six-weekly service between Dallas and Kansas City, was scheduled to depart at 2:25 PM. Due to an initial maintenance delay, and then the subsequent crew argument, the flight was delayed two hours and 24 minutes before the aircraft eventually took off.

The short one-hour, four-minute journey then landed in Kansas City at 6:16 PM that evening, two hours and 21 minutes behind its initial scheduled arrival time of 3:55 PM. The aircraft operating the service was that of a Boeing 737-700, bearing registration N255WN. Full aircraft characteristics are in the table below, as per ch-aviation:

N281WN the carrier's 500th 737 circled over the South Bay as pilots ran emergency checklists and prepared for a single‑engine return.

With the crew member allegedly quitting and refusing to operate the flight, this ultimately forced Southwest to find a replacement crew member to crew the service to Kansas City, and was the primary cause of the flight's delay.

To crew a 737-700, the typical requirements are two pilots and three cabin crew to serve the total of 137 passengers in an all-economy class configuration. Southwest is the largest operator of the 737-700, with more than 270 active aircraft in its fleet.

Delap’s Nottingham Forest stunners hand Manchester City third league defeat in a row

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Wait long enough, and results will come to reflect performances. Manchester City may have won their first seven games of the season, but they always looked vulnerable, and now have lost three in a row. Nottingham Forest may have been second bottom but, other than the twin curses of missing chances giving away daft goals, they seemed to be playing reasonably well. Put the two together, and the outcome was a dramatic regression to the mean. It turns out the stats were right, after all.

Liam Delap scored twice to remind people just what a good striker he can be. Oliver Glasner, after last week’s anger, can breathe a little easier in his job. And Enzo Maresca remains a paradox, a manager obsessive about control whose teams always seem oddly susceptible to direct running.

After last week’s defeat at Brentford, Glasner said he regretted compromising too much in the early weeks of his reign at Forest. Although he gave no precise details as to what that might mean, he elaborated a little on Thursday, speaking of the need for “trust and respect” in the right balance and suggesting there’d been too much trust and not enough respect this season at Forest: too much Woodstock and not enough “high-performance nature of military.”

Glasner’s brave new uncompromising world was ushered in with just two changes to the starting lineup from the defeat at Brentford last week, Ola Aina and Dan N’Doye dropping out for Daniel Muñoz and Ryan Yates, who both came on at half-time last week. It may have been an underwhelming revolution but it soon worked, Jair Cunha dispossessing Josko Gvardiol and running through to opening the scoring after six minutes, Forest’s first home goal since Morgan Gibbs-White’s strike against Bournemouth 34 minutes into the final game of last season, a drought of 510 minutes.

No side this season had underperformed their xG by such a margin as Forest; they began the weekend third in Opta’s expected table but second bottom of the actual table. To an extent that is a matter of misfortune: defeat at Brentford last week, for instance, was a result of a cross and a shot that both looped in off Nikola Milenkovic; when you concede one shot on target but two goals, you know you’ve been unlucky. And when, on Saturday, three minutes after Forest had taken the lead, a Jérémy Doku shot hit Milenkovic and, rather than looping agonisingly past Matz Sels, cannoned wide for a corner, Forest must really have known their luck was in.

One of the biggest issues for Forest this season has been the form of Delap. A gruelling spell at Chelsea had seemingly sapped his confidence but, 18 minutes in, he surged from inside his own half, ran, as Jair had for the opener, between Rúben Dias and Marc Guéhi, and thumped his shot over the dive of Gianluigi Donnarumma. With confidence, it turns out, Delap is a very different player.

Guéhi’s form, meanwhile, is becoming a major concern for City. With Dias wary of Xaver Schlager running to his right and potentially becoming a passing option, it was surely Guéhi’s responsibility to close Delap down. As it was, the pair left a huge channel, inviting Delap to keep going to score his second goal since joining Forest. Guéhi and Gvardiol were both withdrawn at half-time.

In the Guardian app, tap the Profile settings button at the top right, then select Notifications. Turn on sport notifications.

If you already have the Guardian app, make sure you’re on the most recent version.

If you don't have the Guardian app, download it from the iOS App Store on iPhone or the Google Play store on Android by searching for 'The Guardian'.

If James McAtee had not missed the target with two very presentable opportunities, Forest could have been out of sight by half-time. It was never, though, going to be that easy. Seven minutes into the second half, a long diagonal set Doku in behind Muñoz, and although his cross was half-cleared, Rayan Cherki slammed in a brilliant half volley. In a moment, the mood changed. All the anxiety stored up by two months of disappointing form bubbled to the surface again.

Forest may feel that, after the season they have had, they are owed some luck. They were nearly paid back a slice of that when a Yates shot deflected off Elliot Anderson and dropped on to the back of the bar. But still they had burned through a fair amount, with blocks, brave headers and clearances off the line when Gibbs-White set Delap away on the counter. A week ago, it would barely have registered as an opportunity but, self-belief restored, Delap swept into the box and drove his finish in off the far post.

Forest are out of the relegation zone and the idea that City might challenge Arsenal for the title, even if there is no points deduction, is beginning to look a little fanciful.

5 Most Ambitious LitRPG Books of All Time, Ranked by Goodreads

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Thanks to Matt Dinniman's Dungeon Crawler Carl becoming a legitimate phenomenon around the globe, a lot of casual and genre readers are starting to latch onto the massive worlds of LitRPG. Essentially taking a role-playing, progression type of game and bringing it into a literary form, LitRPG has become one of the hottest genres around. He Who Fights With Monsters has started to pick up a ton of steam in the wake of Dungeon Crawler Carl's success, and may very well be the next to get a screen adaptation. But there are so many LitRPG books and series out there to explore, and a lot of them are nothing like the ones you've already heard about or read.

There are some wildly ambitious LitRPG novels out there, each bringing something bold and new to a space that is doing nothing but expanding and evolving by the minute. Some show off what a story like Dungeon Crawler Carl would look like if the protagonist had their children with them for the dangerous event. Others take readers to remote farms on faraway planets as protagonists try and live peaceful lives. There's a genuinely great LitRPG series where the main character is literally a tree.

LitRPG writers have ambition in spades, delivering some awesome stories to check out that you may not be too familiar with. Below, we've picked out five of the genre's most ambitious, and ranked them according to the rating on Goodreads.

This eight-book Isekai adventure from author SpaizZzer just concluded earlier this fall, and it has stood for nearly a decade as one of the most unique stories in the LitRPG space. If you're not familiar, the main character of the series is a tree, and that premise was turned into eight very popular, highly loved books, which feels like a miracle in and of itself.

The first book of the series begins with a man named Matt being reborn into a fantasy world, which is the usual start to many LitRPG and/or Isekai stories. However, when Matt is reborn, he comes back as a sentient tree, unable to move from his spot. He quickly realizes there is more to his situation meets the eye, as the tree becomes the central battleground in a demon incursion, and Matt (aka TreeTree) needs to use his evolving powers to aid the heroes that find themselves beneath his branches.

So many LitRPG stories focus on adults in situations where they become players in some sort of dangerous adventure or death game, forcing them to level up and adapt to save themselves, and potentially their entire worlds. Erin Ampersand's Apocalypse Parenting series makes that entire concept devastatingly personal as it puts a regular mom in the midst of battle — right alongside her three young children.

The name really says it all. Apocalypse Parenting flips the script on the genre by exploring what it would be like to lead your own kids through the kind of crazy, other-worldly scenarios that are common in Dungeon Crawler Carl and countless other LitRPG novels. It makes for not only an emotional journey, but also one that makes you think a lot about the logistics that might plague most characters you encounter in stories like this.

The Wandering Inn is one of the biggest names in the world of LitRPG, and it has a little bit of everything. The story of Erin Solstice has some slice-of-life vibes to it, as she creates a safe harbor for creatures of all kinds, but it's also filled with a ton of great fantasy elements along the way. It checks so many boxes, which is ambitious in its own right, but it's the sheer size of The Wandering Inn that has it on this list.

The Wandering Inn is one of the longest written works in history, with 20 individual installments released over the last eight years. And those individual novels are often so long that the print versions, which finally started seeing the light of day this year, are actually splitting the books into multiple parts. For example, the original Wandering Inn book was made into two print novels consisting of 640 pages and 784 pages, respectively. It takes a lot to make a story interesting for this long, but Pirateaba has done just that.

There are a lot of slice-of-life LitRPG books out there, focusing less on world-ending battles or death games, and more on level systems can bring to the "slower" aspects of living. Seth Ring's Battle Mage Farmer series is the best of both worlds, delivering on the slice-of-life story without abandoning the bigger tales and stakes of the genre.

John Sutton's tale of trying to build a quiet life as a farmer on the outskirts of an empire comes as he tries his best to avoid the war and devastation all around him. He's at conflict from the jump, wanting nothing more than a simple existence but knowing that he's destined for a lot more. It's a brutally relatable conundrum he finds himself in, and it makes Battle Mage Farmer one of the more intriguing LitRPG series out there.

Speaking of warriors living out their days on hopefully peaceful farmland, Beware of Chicken is a layered story that takes a different approach to nearly every kind of tale you'll find in LitRPGs. It's also not at all what many are expecting from progression fantasy, but that's a big reason why it stands out.

The book begins with the protagonist waking up not only in another world, but in the body of a celestially powerful martial arts master. Instead of taking up the conflict that his former body host was a part of, the new Jin decides he'd rather take his powers home and start a farm. It's relaxing, hilarious, and constantly bubbling over with the threat of life outside the homestead.

So far, there are five books in the Beware of Chicken series (the sixth arriving early next year), and the 4.45 Goodreads rating on the first installment is the lowest of the entire series.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Ultron Confirmed Who Marvel's Strongest Avenger Is, But Fans Won't Like The Answer

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Ultron settles the debate and reveals which of the Avengers is truly the most powerful. The debate over who deserves the title of the strongest Avenger has fueled decades of intense arguments in and out of universe. Strength in the Marvel Universe is rarely a simple measurement of how much weight a hero can lift, after all. The answers vary drastically depending on the battlefield, the status of each hero's power, and whether weapons and energy-based power can be weighed equally against sheer physical strength. Scarlet Witch can neutralize Thor using her magic, but Swordsman can technically take down Scarlet Witch if he catches her with her guard down.

Marvel Comics and the Marvel Cinematic Universe have presented several conflicting answers to this question. Wanda Maximoff has reshaped reality and rewritten the lives of everyone in the universe, while Hyperion is strong enough to stop clashing universes using his bare hands, and Sentry effortlessly rips Ares' body in half. The MCU playfully turned this debate into a running joke in Thor: Ragnarok, where Tony Stark’s computer security recognizes Hulk as the "strongest Avenger" while relegating Thor to "Point Break." And yet, Thor and Captain Marvel quickly claim the title in Avengers: Infinity War and Avengers: Secret Wars.

The "strongest Avenger" debate has many possible answers, one of which gains more authority when supported by one of the Avengers' biggest villains.

Avengers: Twilight takes place in a possible future where superheroes have been outlawed following a catastrophic event known as H-Day, which resulted in countless deaths and the destruction of Boston. During a televised discussion, an older Steve Rogers and James Stark, Tony Stark's son, revisit the long-running debate about which Avenger deserves to be called the strongest. The young Stark points out that the individual responsible for the highest death toll was the Hulk, who had been placed under Ultron's control. When discussing the incident, Stark confirms Hulk is the strongest Avenger, as Ultron chose to weaponize the Green Goliath rather than Thor or Captain Marvel to destroy the city.

James Stark's assessment measures strength through destructive potential over isolated displays of power. Thor is a literal god who has fought entities like Hela and Surtur, and Captain Marvel can singlehandedly defeat entire alien armies, but Hulk offers a terrifying level of strength that rises indefinitely alongside his rage. Once Ultron gained control of Banner's alter ego, the robot turned one of the Avengers' best defenders into a living weapon capable of destroying an entire city as a starter feat. While Thor, Captain Marvel, and even Iron Man could technically stop Hulk at his base level, nobody can stop the Green Goliath once he crosses a certain anger threshold.

James Stark's declaration that the Hulk is the strongest Avenger in Avengers: Twilight #1 becomes increasingly difficult to dispute. The most famous example is World War Hulk, when the Green Scar returns to Earth in search of vengeance against the Illuminati and illustrates how he could singlehandedly defeat all of Earth's superheroes in a gladiator tournament. A similar demonstration takes place during Incredible Hulks #632, when Hulk's transformation into Worldbreaker threatens to unleash enough energy to destroy North America. Besides, the Illuminati choose to banish the Hulk to outer space precisely because they understand that he's one of the biggest threats to Earth, and their preemptive decision ends up backfiring.

It's been seen on the page and debated for years: which classic Marvel Comics team would win in a straight-up fight? The Avengers or the X-Men?

More recent Marvel stories have taken this terrifying concept even further. In the new Ultimate Universe, Earth-6160, a considerably weaker Hulk oversees horrific experiments on Gamma Island and leaves the resistance team seemingly dead. On Earth-616, the Infernal Hulk defeats the Avengers, the Fantastic Four, and the X-Men, and he transforms many of them into monsters for his army. Parallel to The Infernal Hulk, Avengers: Armageddon explores a similar nightmare with Thunderbolt Ross, whose Red Hulk transformation turns the longtime Hulk hunter into the harbinger of a potential World War III and destroys an entire city in Europe, just like Avengers: Twilight's Ultron-manipulated Hulk.

Who do you think is the strongest Avenger?

Avengers: Twilight is available from Marvel Comics

Keira Knightley and ‘Black Doves’ Creator Joe Barton Why Helen Should Have Been the One to Kill [SPOILER], That Oner and Hopes for Season 3

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

SPOILER ALERT: This article contains major spoilers for “Black Doves” Season 2, now streaming on Netflix.

Keira Knightley is back as super spy Helen Webb in Season 2 of “Black Doves,” alongside Ben Whishaw as her troubled colleague Sam Young and Sarah Lancashire as her stoic spymaster Reed.

The show, created by Joe Barton, returns with plenty of action, blood and bodies as Reed goes missing, Sam makes a Faustian pact with a shady benefactor and Helen’s secret identity is almost exposed — a threat that’s all the more serious given her new role in Number 10 Downing Street now that her husband, Wallace (Andrew Buchan), has become Prime Minister.

With Season 2 out now on Netflix, Knightley and Barton sat down with Variety to discuss why the showrunner chose to focus on Reed’s backstory this season, how the series differs from Apple TV’s London spy thriller “Slow Horses” — and the one character Knightley really wanted to kill.

Joe Barton: It organically came out the storytelling process of Reed’s backstory, and the idea of the sins of the past coming forward and everyone suffering for them. And I wanted to do a “Berlin in the ‘80s” spy thing.

Keira Knightley: I was gonna say, I think you’ve got a front row seat to that one.

Knightley: [Joe] handed me the script that [said] “You’re shooting the nanny,” and I was like, “But she stays late, what the fuck are you talking about? You don’t shoot her! She never goes home — this is the childcare of dreams.” But she has taken the kids, so we do have to kill the nanny. So we did have that conversation.

Knightley: That’s what I love about it, because life doesn’t stop just because you’ve got other stuff. I think that’s what’s lovely about the whole show, is that as heightened and — I mean this in the best way — ridiculous as the whole thing is, it equally has that very human part of it, and that’s what makes it great, in my opinion. That’s what makes it really fun, and that’s what makes it really interesting.

Barton: She doesn’t have it [in real life].

Knightley: Oh yeah, you did actually do that research.

Barton: The prime minister’s wife doesn’t get a security detail, so it’s very realistic.

Knightley: She’s got a hat and glasses, come on. It’s fine!

Knightley: [To Barton] How much are we balancing reality?

Barton: Well, it’s a tricky balancing act, isn’t it?

Barton: I would say we’re not always balancing it.

Knightley: It’s a foot off the floor. A good foot off the floor, sometimes two feet off the floor.

Barton: A couple of meters off the floor! We get talked about a lot with “Slow Horses,” because obviously [they’re both] London spy shows. I think the big difference between us and them is ostensibly “Slow Horses” takes place in the real world, and I don’t think we do. There are obviously recognizable elements of the real world, but fundamentally it’s more me taking a chapter out of the James Bond or “Mission Impossible” book, where you can have these almost flights of fantasy. We have our cake and eat it too, basically.

Barton: With the prime minister’s wife, my argument is always I don’t actually know what Keir Starmer’s wife looks like.

Knightley: When we were shooting, it was Keir Starmer’s wife [Starmer resigned in June], and we were all like, “Actually, I don’t know her name particularly, and I don’t know what she looks like.” So we were all very pleased about that, because we were like, “See? She could be doing anything.”

Knightley: I didn’t get to, no — he made me shoot the nanny instead! I was like, “That’s not the one I want to kill. I want to kill Dani! Why didn’t you let me kill Dani? You killed Dani, and you didn’t let me do it.”

Barton: Do you know what that was? I wanted to keep Dani alive so that you could kill her, [but] it didn’t make sense to get your character there. But then I was on set with Aggie and we were talking. I was like, “I love working with you, and I think the character is so interesting and there could be more.” And she’s like, “Oh no, the thing is, I’ve just got this new job, so I wouldn’t even be available for another series.”

Barton: I just thought it would be so shocking to do that to poor Neve.

Knightley: I didn’t see that coming. Another one bites the dust.

Knightley: We didn’t have a lot of time to rehearse that sequence, and all of a sudden, Julian [Farino, the director] said, “Actually, I want to shoot it in a oner.” And me and Ambika were like, “But we haven’t learnt the fight, so how are you going to shoot it in a oner?” And [the crew] were like, “Well — good luck! We’ll just try and make it work!” So it was quite an interesting day. We did actually manage to shoot it in a oner.

Barton: It ended up working really well, but it wasn’t always a guarantee, was it?

Knightley: It did end up working out well. It definitely didn’t feel like a guarantee on the day that it would.

Barton: Yeah, we have bits of Christmas. We pop in and out of it.

Knightley: We were shooting over Christmas again, which meant that it was quite hard to avoid at certain points. But actually, when we finally shot in Trafalgar Square, it was post-Christmas, so we did then have to invent the Christmas tree.

Barton: We had to digitally put the tree in. The first [season] we were chasing Christmas, and this one we were running from it. The [real] tree is quite threadbare, so we were like “We could make it even nicer,” and the Norwegian government [who give the Trafalgar Square Christmas tree to the U.K. each year] were like “No, you’re not allowed to, it has to look like [the real thing].”

Barton: Yeah it has to be the same type of tree and you’re not allowed to make it too –

Barton: I’m in a writers’ room for it at the moment, and we’re hoping.

This interview has been edited and condensed.

What we've been playing - "Sniffing out the warehouses on Prawn Island"

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

A few of the things that have us hooked this week.

Well, hello! I am once again wearing the Bertie hat in your usual host's absence, so joyous welcomes to our regular feature in which we talk about what we've been playing in recent times. This week, Marie has been revisiting Zelda - although perhaps not the one you might expect - while Dom is rediscovering Vice City of the 80s. Kelsey is playing some very weird cards, Connor is back in WoW - possibly forever - and I'm equally smitten with a different RPG.

But enough about us, what about you? What have you been playing this week? Do tell all! And don't forget we have an entire archive of previous What We've Been Playings for your leisurely perusal, just in case you forgot what you were playing a year or a month or a week ago. Bye!

Once again, I am finally playing a game from my backlog that I should've played years ago, and that's the delightful albeit dark card game Inscryption. There's so much more to this than you'd first expect, and I'm surprised the community has been able to keep the game's best bits under wraps for so long. Ultimately, nothing could've prepared me for Act 2, and my only real qualm with the whole experience is that I wish Act 2 had its own endless roguelike mode; you'd never be able to pull me away from it.

The game falters a little at the start of Act 3, I'll admit, and I've found myself very frustrated by what is an otherwise relatively easy time. This is down to me missing some rather useful earlier cards that I've since learnt would've made this final stretch much breezier, but oh well. I will still take down the Uberbots one way or another for the love of the game!

I've sunk every free hour I have into World of Warcraft: Forever since its release on Wednesday night. As of writing I am Level 19, waiting for an escort quest to come up in Westfall. So far things seem largely the same as in the original MMO, though the little changes and improvements I've seen have firmly planted a smile on my face.

This includes the new weather system. Like many others I was caught in a snow storm in Dun Morogh this week, a seemingly random environmental transformation that drastically limits view distance and blankets your screen with the cold stuff. It adds a lot, I think, to the fantasy of the game. I'll be playing this all weekend and for the foreseeable future.

I'll let you guess why I'm playing this right now of all times. Ahead of Grand Theft Auto 6's launch, I've made it my unofficial job to internalise the map of Vice City. I'm spending time driving around the strip, familiarising myself with the docklands, going for a few rounds of golf up in Leaf Links, and sniffing out the warehouses on Prawn Island. I'm immensely curious how similar the map is in GTA 6, and can't wait to see what's changed in the interceding 40 years in-universe. It's even better having V-Rock blast Iron Maiden and Twisted Sister at me; over the past few evenings, I've been playing this extra loud so it drowns out the sound of local fireworks for the sake of my poor, terrified dog. I don't think he's a fan of Bruce 'the air raid siren' Dickinson's voice - but it's better than all the apocalyptic banging.

The cold winter nights are drawing in, which calls for blankets, a warm drink, and a nice peaceful game before bed. I don't have an exact reason for going back to Tears of the Kingdom. Perhaps it's me diving into Ocarina of Time that fuelled my curiosity about returning, but what's surprised me the most is there's still so much I didn't see or do in my first 190 hours with it.

I started again, cleared my save file, and it turns out I've completely forgotten most things about the game - which is by no means a complaint! I'm rather enjoying making the little discoveries that are either brand-new to me, or ones that make me go 'AH-HA, I remember you' (in the case of Gloom Hands, the reaction was 'NOPE, NOPE, NOPE, NOPE.')

I've also had another surprise. I play in handheld mode mostly, and ToTK was all looking a bit blurry on the Switch 2's screen. So I got the upgrade pack and suddenly everything became beautiful again - Link was in focus, the individual blades of grass in Hyrule Field were clearly whipping in the wind, and the crispy snow around Rito village almost sparkled. I don't often get upgrade packs for games I already own, but this one was worth it.

I suspect this won't be the last time you see me yapping about Queen's Domain here. I've already enthused about it elsewhere on the site this week, but I've managed to squeeze in a few more hours since then, and it continues to enthrall. Here's a small moment from my most recent go: I'd been fumbling about the Tower of Knowledge for a while at this point, dying slightly more than I'd like to those jabby gargoyle gits and lumbering chimera things, so I thought I'd have a little wander away from the violence and have an explore. That was when I noticed the gap on the bookshelf, wide enough to slot in the mysterious Ratty Bug Compendium I'd been wondering about ever since I picked it up elswhere some five hours early.

Moments later a secret passage had opened, leading out to a balcony overlooking the ocean. Treasure! Exciting! But just as I'm about to go back inside, I notice a little outcrop on the exterior of the tower, seemingly reachable with my sword's magic dashing power. So over I go, discovering an otherwise inaccessible route into an inner chamber. There, I find a lever, which I excitedly pull, only to be rewarded with... nothing at all. Until five minutes or so later, when I discover a secret panel has now opened up on the other side of the tower, granting me access to the treats within. In isolation, it's not a remarkable gaming anecdote, but Queen's Domain is just crammed with these little incremental secrets and surprises, full of clever design that makes exploration feel so rewarding. I've already spotted more enigmas waiting to be cracked, and if Queen's Domain keeps distracting me like this, I might never escape its pull.

Joshua Buatsi and Willy Hutchinson renew their rivalry for the vacant WBC world light-heavyweight title in Manchester on Saturday.

The British rivals first met in a highly entertaining, hard-fought contest on the undercard of Daniel Dubois' win over Anthony Joshua in September 2024.

Londoner Buatsi, 33, twice knocked down Hutchinson before going on to win by split decision.

Scotland's Hutchinson, 28, suffered the second defeat of his professional career that night but two wins since have put him in a position to fight for a first world title.

Can Buatsi repeat his victory? Or will Hutchinson gain revenge in Manchester?

BBC Sport spoke to figures from across the boxing world to get their predictions.

What Poetry Can Teach Us About Wonder: An Interview With Maya C. Popa

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

The most trusted resource for mindfulness and meditation.

An interview with poet Maya C. Popa on attention, uncertainty, and the art of remaining curious.

We often associate wonder with extraordinary experiences, moments of beauty or awe that stop us in our tracks. But what if wonder is also a way of paying attention, one that invites us to question what we think we know and remain open to what we cannot immediately understand?

Poetry offers a particular way of exploring this possibility. It asks us to slow down, look more closely, and make room for ambiguity, uncertainty, and experiences that resist easy explanation. In doing so, poetry may have something to teach us about presence, curiosity, and our capacity to find meaning in the world around us.

What if wonder is a way of paying attention that invites us to question what we think we know and remain open to what we cannot immediately understand?

Poet Maya C. Popa has spent years exploring wonder through her doctoral research and her creative work. Her collection Wound Is the Origin of Wonder examines wonder beyond beauty and astonishment, while her newest collection, If You Love That Lady, explores the ways attention and experience can challenge our assumptions. In this conversation, Popa reflects on the relationship between poetry and mindfulness, the value of questioning our perceptions, and how cultivating wonder might deepen our connection to the world around us.

US: Giant pandas from China make public debut at Atlanta zoo

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Ping Ping and Fu Shuang are the first giant pandas to be housed in Zoo Atlanta after a gap of two years.

Two giant pandas from China have made their first public appearance at a zoo in the US city of Atlanta as a queue of visitors lined up to see the bears.

In a treat for them and their fans, Ping Ping and Fu Shuang —  male and female, respectively — were seen munching on bamboo at Zoo Atlanta on Friday.

The six-year-old pandas arrived from China's Chengdu on September 27.

The FedEx flight they traveled on was dubbed the "Panda Express."

The panda duo had spent the last few weeks getting acclimated to their habitat.

Ping Ping and Fu Shuang have taken up residence in a panda enclosure at the zoo that had been vacant since 2024.

A 25-year-long partnership that saw Atlanta host four pandas on loan since 1999 ended in the midst of strained US-China ties. Seven panda cubs that were born at the zoo during that period now live in Chengdu, China.

That arrangement has been renewed under a 10-year loan agreement following a Washington summit between US President Donald Trump and his Chinese counterpart Xi Jinping.

China's ambassador to the US, Xie Feng, said at a press conference on Friday that the pandas' arrival "marks the renewal of a decade-long bond between China and Zoo Atlanta."

Raymond King, CEO of Zoo Atlanta, described pandas as "animals that have got a unique ability to inspire curiosity, instill passion, evoke joy, and unite people around the globe."

"This extraordinary spirit of partnership helps create a future for a species that is truly beloved the world over,"  he said as the two adult pandas made their debut.

China has a history of leasing the iconic pandas to other countries in a bid to build diplomatic ties, dubbed "panda diplomacy."

Cubs born in foreign zoos are typically sent back to China.

Meanwhile, unaware of the soft power they wield, Ping Ping and Fu Shuang, lounged about their new habitat on Friday.

The zoo recently renovated its panda facilities and has also ensured a long-term supply of locally grown bamboo for its newest residents.

Three US zoos now house the black-and-white mammals.

Dungeon Crawler Carl: Allies & Enemies Officially Releases This Month

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

One of the biggest breakout stars in the world of literature in the last few years is Dungeon Crawler Carl. Matt Dinniman's fantasy series started life as a self-published passion project in 2019, but was subsequently picked up by Amazon and blew up massively around 2024. Now, in 2026, Dungeon Crawler Carl is a bona-fide hit franchise with multiple adaptations on the way alongside a ninth book.

I can see why Dungeon Crawler Carl resonates with audiences the way it has. The books feel like a traditional role-playing game come to life, set in a world where Earth has been destroyed by aliens and human survivors are forced into an 18-level underground labyrinth to compete in an intergalactic reality television show. It's a fairly simple pitch on paper, but the Hunger Games meets Dungeons & Dragons, vibes are boosted by the humor and world-building that make Carl and friends feel truly alive.

Now, Renegade Game Studios has developed a full tabletop adaptation based on the concept of Dungeon Crawler Carl, where one person acts as the game master, controlling the monsters and enemies while other players fight to survive. Of course, any good tabletop RPG requires miniatures, and there's a new set releasing this month, called Allies & Enemies.

"Enhance your gameplay of the Dungeon Crawler Carl roleplaying games with these 10 pre-painted Dungeon Crawler Carl miniatures," reads the official product description. "This set features many of the most dangerous and friendly characters from the bestselling LitRPG book series ready to use in your RPG campaigns or simply to collect! Inside you’ll find Brynhild's daughters: Hekla & Eva, Bianca & Prepotente and even Lucia Mar in both her day and night forms!"

Also releasing this mointh is the Mobs of Mordecai set, and the Unstoppable set, both of which feature their own unique pre-painted miniatures. The full RPG launches November 2026, and the 300-page rulebook is also available to pre-order now. The Dungeon Crawler Carl Roleplaying Game Core Rulebook is 300+ pages focusing on world dungeon floors 3 to 5. The book talks players through character creation, skills, spells, neighborhoods, saferooms, loot, mobs, and all the other essentials. You can build your own character from the ground up with nearly infinite combinations of backgrounds, skills, races, and classes, so there's plenty of room for a proper Dungeon Crawler Carl experience.

The next Dungeon Crawler Carl book, currently under the working title The Beautiful Place, doesn't have an official release date at the time of writing, but we do know it'll be the first part of an epic two-part conclusion, with the finale coming in book 10.

Microplastics have been found in our bodies and the food we eat—and even in remote environments around the world, including seawater and ice in the Arctic. Despite their prevalence, little is known about how they move through the atmosphere. New CIRES-led research, which demonstrates a new method for sampling microplastics in the atmosphere using an uncrewed aerial system (UAS), aims to change that. The findings are published in the journal Scientific Reports.

How Stephen Miller Schemed His Way to the Top

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

President Donald Trump on Friday announced that Stephen Miller, one of his most powerful and politically aggressive aides, would be his next White House chief of staff following disastrous results for Republicans in the midterm elections.

Miller, whose titles until now were deputy chief of staff for policy and homeland security adviser, has orchestrated the most polarizing policies of Trump’s second term—most notably a brutal crackdown on immigration that has claimed the lives of US citizens.

Trump made the change public in a statement on his Truth Social platform, writing that Miller would take over from Susie Wiles on Monday—less than a week after Republicans lost the House and Senate in a searing rebuke of Trump’s policy agenda.

In recent days, Trump has privately blamed Wiles for the worst Republican midterm performance in decades, according to two sources familiar with the matter. Wiles submitted her resignation on Thursday, the sources say.

Trump broadly considered three candidates to replace Wiles, the sources say: special envoy Steve Witkoff, director of national intelligence Jay Clayton, and Miller.

But with the looming threat of congressional investigations starting next year, Trump settled on Miller, seemingly because of his zeal in defending the president and his deep loyalty. Miller has been with Trump since his first campaign and stood by him through his political exile in 2021.

Miller had made his interest for the job known inside the West Wing for weeks, ever since Wiles weathered a series of negative stories before the midterms that suggested her job was at risk, the sources say.

In recent months, Trump also started cutting Wiles out of certain decisions, which contributed to the sense among her detractors that she was on shaky ground, sources say. For instance, Trump reportedly hired Katie Zacharia as press secretary without consulting Wiles, though the White House pushed back on the claim.

With Wiles’ departure, White House aides were bracing for further personnel changes. Among other names, Wiles’ deputies James Blair, who put together the GOP midterm strategy, and Richard Walters are seen as vulnerable to being fired by Miller unless they resign first, sources say.

The changing of the guard also is likely to bring respite to defense secretary Pete Hegseth, who has had a strained relationship with Wiles as of late but is seen as ideologically aligned with Miller, sources say.

Hegseth and Miller worked closely last year when the Trump administration began a campaign of strikes against boats around Venezuela. Miller, who also leads the Homeland Security Council within the White House, orchestrated the campaign through the Pentagon.

Wiles leaves the White House as arguably the most successful chief of staff to Trump, who infamously cycled through chiefs in his first term. By comparison, Wiles kept Trump’s confidence from the start of his second presidential campaign in 2021.

Still, Wiles weathered a number of challenges in recent months. She underwent a successful course of chemotherapy, but also faced accusations that her daughter had received lucrative contracts in part because of the family’s connection to the president. (The White House and Wiles’ daughter’s firm pushed back on these claims.)

“Serving President Trump in the White House has been the honor of a lifetime,” Wiles wrote on X. “I’m so proud to have been a part of what we’ve accomplished together from securing our border and cutting taxes to strengthening our economy and putting America First.”

Critical Role Officially Returns to Its Roots for New Campaign with Hololive

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

All the details about Shattered Legends, the new Critical Role x Hololive Daggerheart series

Announced in October, Shattered Legends is an upcoming miniseries featuring Critical Role and members of Hololive Production. Critical Role founders Matt Mercer and Marisha Ray will team up with Hololive’s Nerissa Ravencroft, Mori Calliope, Shiori Novella, and Gigi Murin​​​​ for a new adventure set in the world of Exandria, where the Critical Role story began with Campaign 1: Vox Machina.

Shattered Legends will premiere on Nov. 13 at 6 p.m. PT / 9 p.m. ET on Nerissa Ravencroft's YouTube channel and on Critical Role's streaming platform, Beacon. The six-episode actual play show will use the rules for Daggerheart, the tabletop role-playing game designed by Critical Role's publishing studio, Darrington Press. Mercer will take the role of Game Master, while Ray will sit on the players' side of the table along with the Hololive crew, helping them navigate a treacherous new adventure in Exandria.

Shattered Legends is set in the Shattered Teeth archipelago, located southeast of Tal'dorei, the continent where the first Critical Role campaign, Vox Machina, takes place. The adventurers will explore the island of Ubradar, and more specifically a mysterious location known as the Undermaw, "a massive sinkhole with an unknown darkness sealed below," according to a news release. If this reminds you of the popular anime Made in Abyss, the similarities don't end there. In the Shattered Teeth islands, time flows differently than in the outside world, just like in Made in Abyss' eponymous giant hole in the ground.

The characters and classes for each of the players in Shattered Legends have also been revealed. Marisha Ray plays tidekin witch Florence, "a moon witch of the sea who believes she guides the souls of lost sailors to the afterlife of the Sister Divas." Nerissa Ravencroft plays elf rogue Siliul 'Sil' Faeli, "a noble turned rogue and skilled tracker seeking refuge on Ubradar." Mori Calliope plays infernis bard Kaz Oogod, "the last of the Oogod clan, a young musician with a 'world-changing instrument.'" Shiori Novella plays fungrin druid Marchen 'March' Rosemyne, "an orphan searching for the Huntsman she lives with, aided by her connection to nature and a network of mushrooms." Finally, Gigi Murin plays faerie seraph Giryn, "a devoted follower of Mother Liopé on a mission to spread the Enclave’s teachings and witness the nature of life and death."

The Hololive cast members will appear with new virtual avatars based on the spectacular design of their characters, as seen in the presentation video below. Mercer and Ray will appear in the flesh, even if many fans are complaining this was a missed opportunity to give them a virtual avatar.

Shattered Legends will not end with the six-episode miniseries. A promotional tour will hit conventions in North America, Europe, and Asia, starting with a panel at Anime Frontier in Fort Worth, Texas, on Dec. 5.

If you miss Tal'dorei and the setting of the adventures of Vox Machina, or if you're just curious to see how a group of cheery VTubers will react to Mercer's evil Game Master shenanigans, tune in to Shattered Legends when it premieres on Nov. 13.

With the ‘New’ Yeezy, Ye Says He Wants to ‘Let the Clothes Do the Talking.’ But Who’s Listening?

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

“I don’t have a lot of words tonight,” Ye told me, not long after concluding his Yeezy Season 01 runway show in Los Angeles on Wednesday evening. “I’ll let the clothes do the talking.”

You might read that quote and be surprised to hear that Ye said it warmly, with an all-32-bared smile. But for those still paying attention, his relatively upbeat demeanor tracks with the tame-not-turnt persona that Ye has performed through most of 2026; the one that’s been slowly-but-surely trying to reamass the cultural capital he’s spent most of the last decade either annihilating or reclaiming. Legions of fans have proven they’re still all in; the culture at large remains justifiably dubious. The question this week is, how will Ye’s return to fashion affect the calculus?

Upon arrival, a typical Hollywood warehouse exterior turned into a Frank Herbert-esque interior as soon as you crossed the threshold. Inside, a hundred or so guests sat on two rows of unbroken benches that encircled a large white mound in the center, lit by a heavenly white glow from above. Inside was a mix of the usual cool kids: models (including the runway-favorite teen rapper Nettspend) and recurring characters in the overarching, enduring Kanye Universe. There was Fear of God founder Jerry Lorenzo, who made conversation with Don C, while 2 Chainz was patiently posted up in a hooded black leather coat like ’98 Wesley Snipes. Big Sean, rebuffing the sweltering indoor temperatures in a shearling jacket over a crewneck sweater, caught up with Hit-Boy.

Models on the Yeezy Season 01 runway, which featured a characteristically minimalist set piece.

The 19-year-old rapper Nettspend walked in the show.

Once everyone was seated, the lights dimmed as the unmistakable opening piano note of “Runaway” set the show into motion, with one lone model striding out in a killer black trench. From there, two more models, men this time, walked out in the opposite direction; what followed was a bit of organized chaos with the number of models and paths alternating at random intervals to the tune of a playlist that zipped between new Ye music (UK garage vibes, it would seem) and a gothic orchestra not unlike the scores Danny Elfman used to chef up for Tim Burton.

The fits were crisp, practical and tactical. Most of the menswear was ready-made for basic maneuvers, while the women’s looks were sleek and casual, with a few Milla Jovovich-in-Fifth-Element skimpy ensembles thrown in because Ye can only restrain himself but so much. The most notable thing, about the guys especially, were the relatively streamlined silhouettes: close-cropped coats, bombers and vests, fitted pants and sweats. Where past Ye lines championed and emblazoned the path for the baggy wave, with oversized boots and pants you could swim in, Ye seemed to be brazenly planting a flag back on the opposite end of the spectrum.

Nearly 12 years, and a dozen lifetimes, since his debut Yeezy Season 1 runway show in 2015, the notion of a “Season 01”—his first formal showing since his damning 2022 show in Paris, the one with the “White Lives Matter” shirts—is a conspicuous attempt to clear the slate. All of his previous shows had been produced in collaboration with Adidas, who severed ties with Ye four years ago, after he tweeted a tirade of antisemetic remarks. This time around, to let the clothes tell it, Ye is locked in. I don’t have an image of every single Yeezy Season line seared into my brain, but I feel comfortable saying Ye’s latest was the first time I looked at one of his collections and objectively thought, I could wear that. Most of the gear is quite solid—and familiar Ye's running the same playbook with Season 01 that he’s run with his music, and to some degree, his persona. It’s the Yeezy gear you know and love(d), refined to make it that much more palatable, similar to the way a song like “Whatever Works” puts him back over chipmunk’d soul samples. And true to the title, it does indeed; the fact that it does is maybe a testament to the brilliance that got lost in the insanity. But is playing the hits (quite literally, with “Runaway” opening the show) what we want from one of the most paradigm-shifting—and absolutely chaotic—creative minds of our time?

Historically, Ye’s designs are so focused on an idea of utilitarianism that they ultimately come off as a bit drab, if not downright dystopian. Essentials like hoodies and beefy leather jackets are still central to his vision, but as the models strode around in his usual earth-toned palette—save for a handful of black looks to start, and a bright pop of reds at the end—it became clear that Ye was putting on the same show in a different kind of stadium. The same sci-fi silhouettes, tweaked to their cleanest shape and most pleasing hue, sans screenprinted hate speech. Ideologically, the runway show wasn’t far off from the experience of hearing his 2007 hit “Good Life” all these years later, ringing off stadium speakers, carrying 80,000 people along on a wave of wistfulness— even if Ye himself only performed approximately 75% of the lyrics against a backing track.

In keeping with all of his other moves this year, Yeezy Season 01 is Ye presenting a safer, rule-abiding version of himself: Ye with just art, no antics. Much in the way that his latest album, Bully, eschewed any lyrics that say, shouted out R. Kelly or Bill Cosby (which is more than can be said about 2024’s Vultures 1), it felt like a deep sigh of relief to see him present clothes that don’t feature appalling slogans, at a fashion show with Candace Owens nowhere in sight. But the compromise for a sanitized Kanye is also one that can’t help but feel anodyne as well. The New Ye doesn’t needle, but he’s lost Old Ye’s knack for trying something new. Of course, we’re also well over a decade into the Yeezy experiment—at this point, the present has caught up to the futuristic vision Ye first dreamt up back in 2015. There’s a whole new generation of aspiring designers creating in his image, kids who fancy themselves trailblazers whose paths just loop back to Ye: streamer Kai Cenat just launched his own clothing brand, Vivet, while N3on attended Wednesday night’s show, skipping the Yeezy space gleefully documenting every interaction. It feels as though all of this has happened before, and all of it will happen again.

Mystery. Intrigue. Chaos. This is the typical life cycle for a Ye project, be it music, fashion, or otherwise. An announcement materializes, hype ensues. Then, inevitably, the seams start to show and it becomes apparent that he isn’t inviting you on a pre-planned journey so much as building the plane in real time, seconds before takeoff. And then, through sheer force of will, or maybe even genius, the result ends up so captivating that the speedbumps hardly matter. He’s still a master showman, and all it takes is a strong yearning for the Old Ye to find yourself surrendering to it. The efficacy of that last factor, though, has undeniably waned over the years. For much of the 2020s, in the midst of many a manic outburst or worse, Ye’s messes have hardly cohered into something objectively beautiful. The intrigue curdled into perverse bemusement; for every unwavering diehard fan, there was a rubbernecker tapped in to watch the circus.

The 2026 version of Ye has been an exercise in proving how many people will show up when he shuts up, and whether he can make it back from the brink if he delivers the right spectacle.

The result is the most well-oiled the Yeezy Machine has ever been. An album that more or less dropped when he said it would, for once. A smattering of live shows across the world, each in a sold-out stadium boasting record-breaking rap concert attendance where he leans on his most enduring strengths: visual spectacle and a bulletproof catalog. The only area left to renegotiate is fashion…which brings us back to Wednesday night in LA.

These are the scenes you’d expect at a Ye event, but the first sign that the overall vibe was different and more relaxed was that the man himself was at the door, tucked off into a hallway, immaculately dressed in a fitted all-black leather fit offset by a ridiculously large and ornate gold waist fixture—every bit the Dune-caliber fit as the space we were standing in required. Ahead of the show, he posed backstage flanked by his wife, Bianca Censori, and the four kids he shares with his ex, Kim Kardashian (including 13-year-old North, who was sporting her unmistakable blue-raspberry locks). At Yeezy events past, you’d expect to see harried staff scrambling, while Ye was somewhere out of sight but exuding stress and anxiety out into the atmosphere. But on Wednesday, the 49-year-old beamed as friends made their way past him to their seats. Even a three-piece-suited Kid Cudi came over to say hello.

Ye backstage with his children, from left: North, Psalm, Chicago, and Saint West.

Fifteen years ago, Yeezy Season 01 would’ve caused a massive sea change in the menswear world almost instantaneously, with the wave starting in SoHo then slowly emanating out into the beyond. In 2026, does Kanye still move that needle? The Year in Ye has been one big display of his ability to Don Draper the culture at large, and shock us all by “how much it never happened.” There’s no controversy big enough to keep him away for too long and very few fences he can’t mend.

For example, Sean and Cudi ceased their umpteenth falling out with their old label boss two months ago at a guest-packed, blockbuster homecoming show in Chicago on Ye’s tour stop; their show of support tonight only underlined how deeply the hatchet has been buried yet again. When I spoke to Big Sean after the show, he could only marvel at how much Ye has “given” to the culture across all different categories. One-time GOOD Music COO Steven Victor said the show made him feel like Ye “has his shit together,” noting that Ye admitted to him that he was still working out the model patterns hours before showtime. In past Yeezy Seasons, the cram-effect would’ve been apparent, but here, Victor said he wouldn’t have noticed because it all seemed to go off so effortlessly.

As for the man himself? I spoke to him, but again, he didn’t have much to say, at least on the record. He seemed excited that I picked up on the intentional disharmony of the model routes, and explained that the space we were standing in was a “language I’ve been working on for 10 years” called “DROAM,” which refers to the minimalist design he hopes to apply to stores, urban planning, and a home for himself.

“I got a quote for you,” Victor interjected. “The king returns!” Ye laughed. The question remains, though: Is he welcome?

The Yangwang U9 Xtreme Just Set Two Guinness World Speed Records

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

The electric Chinese supercar went more than 312 mph on a public road in the Abu Dhabi desert.

The fastest production car in the world. No, not necessarily an attribute that would be useful in your real life, unless your driveway is a section of unrestricted autobahn, but as an achievement, it's something automakers love to boast about. Porsche. Ferrari. Bugatti. And now Yangwang.

The luxury division of Chinese giant BYD (as Lexus is to Toyota), Yangwang's appropriately named U9 Xtreme hypercar just hit 312 mph on a public road in Abu Dhabi. This feat nets it two Guinness World Records at once: the single-direction highest speed for both a production car and for a production electric car.

At this point, perhaps the electric production speed record doesn't need its own subdivision, especially since the U9 was chiefly breaking its own previous record of 308 mph, set last year. The U9 Xtreme, or U9X, has four electric motors, one at each wheel, for a grand total of over 3000 hp. That's three Veyrons concentrated into a single car.

See all results for used cars for sale near 34472

The battery architecture is also distinct from the standard U9, with a 1200-volt architecture and the ability to both send and receive huge amounts of power with greatly improved thermal efficiency. Even when nearly depleted, at 20 percent charge, it can still pump out 1800 kilowatts of power (2414 hp), or soak up 700 kilowatts through regenerative braking.

A motor at each corner means almost limitless fine-tuning for torque-vectoring control, meting out all that power with minimal wheelslip. The U9X also features electric dampers that clamp down on squat and dive under hard acceleration and braking, and active aerodynamic elements to maintain stability at speed. A tip of the hat also goes to Indonesia's Giti Tires, who developed the rubber capable of putting up with these tremendous speeds and forces.

Yangwang only plans to build thirty of these U9X models, and it's not like you can just slap on a license plate and bully 911 Turbos on the Autobahn. The 4.35-mile stretch of public road used for the record attempt was closed at the time, and also fastidiously cleaned of debris, including going back over the surface looking for any wires left by the street sweepers.

However, as a demonstration of just how advanced BYD's battery chemistry can be, this new speed record represents all kinds of bragging rights. Speed records come and go. For now, nothing can touch the Yangwang U9 Xtreme.

Report: Surprise €10m deal tabled for Liverpool veteran

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Liverpool have a lot of decisions that they need to make on their squad over the next few months.

A handful of their first-team squad all have their contracts expiring in June 2027 and, with no negotiations lined up for any of them, another massive overhaul could be just around the corner.

Of course, the number of transfers that Liverpool have been linked with is obscene but, at least it proves that the Reds are casting their net far and wide for their next transfers.

Some positions are obviously more in need of reinforcement than others but, since Liverpool could end up moving some of their own players on as well, nobody actually knows what the next year or so could look like for the Anfield outfit.

That is, especially when multi-million deals are put up for grabs.

AC Milan want to offer Virgil van Dijk €10 million per year to join them from Liverpool

Virgil van Dijk has easily been one of Liverpool's best players ever since he joined.

No matter who he's been partnered with or who the Reds have faced, the Dutchman has always managed to come out as one of the stand-out performers.

Indeed, things have gone a bit wrong for him every now and then but, more often than not, he's proven why he's one of the world's best centre-backs.

However, now that he's 35 years old, questions are being asked about how long he can keep these performance levels up for.

Liverpool have already started to line up players such as Giovanni Leoni and Jeremy Jacquet to replace him but, right now, Andoni Iraola's men would be lost without their captain in the starting XI.

So, that's why the latest update from MilanNews24 is so surprising.

"According to what MilanNews24 has learned, Cardinale would like to make a concrete attempt," they wrote.

"It's not just a tempting idea or a name linked to AC Milan to fuel the fans' ambitions: the Rossoneri owner is reportedly willing to seriously evaluate the conditions for bringing the Dutchman to Italy.

"To land Van Dijk, there are two key conditions. The first concerns Champions League qualification , a crucial element in presenting the defender with an ambitious and competitive sporting project.

"The second is naturally economic: a two-year contract worth 8-10 million euros per season would be on the table .

"A significant figure, which testifies to the scope of the investment envisioned by Cardinale.

"AC Milan is therefore dreaming of an international signing for the summer of 2027. Van Dijk would be a signing capable of bringing experience, leadership, and personality. Now they need to turn that dream into a concrete possibility."

Virgil van Dijk is still one of Liverpool's most important players

As mentioned, Van Dijk is key to the way that Liverpool play at the moment.

When he's left on the bench for a cup tie or a less important game, the difference in quality is staggering.

The Dutchman may not seem like he does all that much when he's on the pitch but, in reality, he's the glue that holds the team together.

Of course, other players such as Ronald Araujo or Joe Gomez can try and plug the gap left by the Dutchman but, neither of them are anywhere near his level.

It was always going to be a worry that Van Dijk would leave for free at the end of this season but, the fact that a concrete bid is about to come from AC Milan is surprising.

Most reports that emerge from Italy make it clear that most Serie A clubs are skint with several outfits often linked with haggling Liverpool for a discount on Federico Chiesa's impending transfer.

So, the fact that AC Milan are willing to give Van Dijk around €10 million per year is shocking.

His stature and name in the game will obviously carry a lot of weight but, unless you're a team who knows you can afford his wages such as Liverpool, it feels a tad strange for him to be targeted by anyone else.
